# D5: the node's side of the passive network sensor (P4-4-A5, revised for option (b) under P4-4-L3)

D5 wrote this note on 2026-11-07 for brief P4-4-A5. **D5 revised it on 2026-11-11 for brief P4-4-L3** (`docs/agent-handoffs.md`, "Phase 4 item 4, second part", D4-86), after the owner chose option (b) (D4-85): the sensor is a separate Linux host at each site, and the node dials it. **This text answers the reviews of `a50ee61c` and of `4d03075a`:** D4's B1 and B2, D3's L1 to L4 and its three changes, D6's B1 to B7 and its four points, `records-auditor`'s points, and D1's rulings on #628 and on C13 0.2 (#627). §1.16 maps each finding to the section that answers it. **Amended on 2026-11-12 for brief P4-4-L5** (D4-88): the 14 amendments that D3's #631 lists for this note, after D3's note and the threat model were amended for C13 0.2 (`docs/phase4/D3-sensor-link.md`, merged as `3176c60f`); §1.16 names where each went. **Amended on 2026-11-13 for brief P4-4-L6** (D4-90): D4's cross-review of this note (comment 6835796775 on #633, B1 to B5 and its notes), D5's own cross-review of C13 0.2 where it changes this note (comment 6835767712 on #634, B1, B2, B4 and B5 and its notes), D1's rulings in D4-90, and D3's rulings in #641 at `01cc3b40` (SL-31, SL-30's run file that is behind, SL-R6's carry-over, the gap rule in SL-16 and C8-7) with D3's comment 6836722860 on #634; matched to #641 at `e164e416` (D3's answers to D6's reviews, from B1 to B4 at `467c1dd6` to the review of `50a67730`, with D1's ruling on the residual at `bb7f0d91` and D3's answers to D5's three points and to D5's point on the tie) and to #645 at `1c454cb6` with D1's rulings on #645 of 2026-11-13. §1.16 maps each finding to its answer, and §1.11 lists the values this note shares with C13 0.2.

- **Part 1** is the node's side under option (b), a design for D1's approval. Its requirements are D3's rules (`docs/phase4/D3-sensor-link.md`, SL-1 to SL-29 and SL-R1 to SL-R8), C13 0.2 as proposed in #627 and D1's rulings. **C13 0.2 is cited as `docs/phase4/D4-c13-0.2-proposal.md` (#627)** (clauses CH-1 to CH-47), by file and PR, not by head (D1's ruling). The two documents were reviewed together; the pair of heads that every reviewer passed is recorded in D1's merge comment on each PR. **D1's rulings are cited where they are recorded:** "ruling N on #628" is D1's comment "Reviews of `a50ee61c`, and D1's rulings" on #628 (rulings 1 to 15); "recorded ruling N" is D1's comment "Rounds two and three, and D1's further rulings" on #628 (comment 6803459738, rulings 1 to 4); "ruling N on #627" is D1's comment "Reviews of `674ce453`, and D1's rulings" on #627 (rulings 1 to 8), whose table of the twelve differences is cited as "difference N"; D1's later rulings on #627 are its comments "Round two at `873c0eb5`" (6803089547) and "Rounds three to five, and D1's ruling on CH-40" (6803460354); and "ruling N on #631" is D1's comment 6804993239 on #631 (rulings 1 to 8). Where this design and C13 0.2 still differ, §1.11 lists it for D1. Each item names the code on `main` it changes and the test that would show it, with its control.
- **Part 2** is the note of 2026-11-07. The sections written for a sensor on the node, over a named pipe with a service SID, are marked **History**: they apply again only if option (a) or (c) is reopened. The sections that still hold say what option (b) changes in them.

**Evidence.** Everything in this note is `desk`. A statement about what exists names the file and function it was read from, on `main` at `551b03dd`. Every other statement is a requirement and is **not built**. No test was run for this note, nothing was installed, and no device or network was contacted. Nothing is `hardware-verified`. A figure marked **unmeasured** is a choice with a stated reason, not a measurement.

# Part 1: the node's side under option (b) (P4-4-L3)

## 1.0 Findings about `main`

D5 found four ways, D6 five more, D5 a tenth on the second read, and D3 an eleventh (F11, ruling 1 on #631 (comment 6804993239)), in which a sensor's work on the node can reach SER collection, Syslog receive, forwarding or the node's start. D3 and D6 each read the functions and agree that F1 to F9 are real, and `records-auditor` checked all nine against `origin/main`. **Each is `desk`, read on `main` at `551b03dd`, and none was reproduced by a run. No record can arrive from a sensor today:** no code outside the tests calls `ServeSensor`, and `ServeSensor` is the only caller of `sensorlink.Link.Serve` (`platform/node/sensor.go`). The tests that call `ServeSensor` are `platform/node/*_test.go`, and D6's `tests/security/sensor_status_boundary_d6_test.go`, `sensor_unreachable_d6_test.go` and `detector_before_clock_d6_test.go`. So the intake that is merged has nothing that feeds it. **But F2, F5 to F7, F9 and F10 are reachable by configuration alone, on `main` today** (D6, and D1's comment 6803782160): they need only a `network_sensor` section in the node's configuration, with no sensor at all, and `main` accepts that section, though only with `evidence_class` `simulated` (`checkNetworkSensor`). For F2, `Start` (`runtime.go`) returns the error of `startSensor`, which runs whenever the section is set and fails when the hold's records cannot be read (`loadIntake`) or recovered (`Link.RecoverHold`); for F7, the same `Start` returns the error of `startDB` for the network database, which exists only with the section. Both need the section and nothing more.

| | Found by | File and function | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | D5 | `platform/node/missed.go` `heldMissed`; `schedule.go` `runOnce`; `layout.go` (the health stream in the evidence database, class `platform`); `contracts/store/class.go` (`platform` is refuse-new) | While an entry's `collection.missed` can't be written, its runs are not sent. A sensor's health events that filled the platform class would stop SER collection. Today the sensor writes `intake.drop-counted` (`sensor.go` `reportDrops`) and the network database's store alarms (`runtime.go` `storeAlarm`, `Tick`) there |
| F2 | D5 | `platform/node/runtime.go` `Start`, `startSensor` (`sensor.go`) | A fault in the intake's start (`loadIntake`, `Link.RecoverHold`) fails the node's start |
| F3 | D5 | `runtime.go` `Tick`, `tickSensor` (`sensor.go`), `tickDetector` and `observeTo` (`netdetector.go`) | The sensor's work, with a detector drive that has no bound, runs inside `Tick`, before `Prune` and the status write |
| F4 | D5 | `netintake.go` `dispatchHold`, `resultHold`, `abandonHold`; `platform/sensorlink/hold.go` `Dispatch`, `Result`, `Abandon`; `link.go` `observe` | The scheduler's run path takes `Link.cmu`, which the read loop holds through each network commit and each hold save |
| F5 (D6 B1a) | D6 | `runtime.go` `Tick`, `view` | `Tick` holds `r.state`'s write lock across `c5.Resume` of a blocked or drain-only database, up to `StartupDeadline` (5 min). Every `Log`, `Store` and `Mode` call takes the read lock through `view`: the scheduler's run path, and the Syslog intake's appends |
| F6 (D6 B1b) | D6 | `prune.go` `Prune`, `pruneDB`; `schedule.go` `cannotKeep` | One mutex, `pruneMu`, over every database, and `pruneDB` loops while a class is above target. `cannotKeep` calls `Prune` on the run path, **only when adapter-evidence is near its limit** (its used bytes plus `QuarantineRoom` above its quota), which is when collection most needs it |
| F7 (D6 B1c) | D6 | `runtime.go` `startDB`, `adopt`; `Start` | A database that opens and then fails a read fails the node's start; each database's startup runs in turn before any collection |
| F8 (D6 B1d) | D6 | `suspend.go` `pruneConsumers` | **A property of the design proposed here, not of `main`:** `main` has no `node-sensors` consumer. #628's first text added one, and a required audit consumer whose cursor stops would hold the audit stream; a full audit class stops credentialed collection (D1-11, `TestAuditFullSendsNothing`) |
| F9 (D6 B1e) | D6 | `runtime.go` `modeChanged`, `Tick` | Each mode change of any database writes a `store.mode_change` audit record and a health event, with no cap |
| F10 | D5, second read | `platform/node/forwarding.go` `NewForwarding`, `liveStore.db`; `modules/telemetry/forward/forward.go` (the send loop over `cfg.Streams`, and `peek`) | Each target has one forwarder session over every stream, the sensor's alert stream among them. `liveStore` returns an error when a stream's database is not open, `peek` returns it, and the send loop returns it, which ends that target's session for **every** stream. So a sensor database that is not open stops that target's forwarding of SER and Syslog events too (D6's item 2 on `4d03075a` reached the same path through `Forwarding.Check`) |
| F11 | D3, on #631 (ruling 1 on #631 (comment 6804993239)); confirmed by D6 and D5 | `modules/security/nodekey/register.go` (`Parse`, `Validate`: one bad entry refuses the whole register); `platform/node` `NewForwarding` and `NewListening`, which resolve the register at the node's start | **A property of this design's first text, not of `main`:** §1.1 as merged put the link key in the node key register as a kind of its own. One faulty link-key entry, or a rollback to a build that does not know that kind, would then refuse the whole register, and with it every target's and listener's key, and so the node's start. `main` has no link key. Closed by the link key's own file (§1.1, §1.9 item 11) |

Also read for this note (`desk`): `Runtime.Collecting` iterates every database, but no product code calls it, so it reaches nothing. And no file under `platform/node`, `platform/sensorlink` or `cmd/otsec` calls `recover`, so a panic in the sensor's work, today inside `Tick`, ends the node (D6; §1.9 item 9).

**D1's ruling on #628 (comment 6803782160, "A correction to D1's record of the couplings, and what holds until they are closed"), which replaces the earlier "they gate the dialer", widened to F11 by ruling 1 on #631 (comment 6804993239), with its fourth point as ruling 8 on #631 words it:**

1. **Until F1 to F11 are closed, no configuration outside the tests carries a `network_sensor` section.**
2. **It is a release blocker:** no release ships before F1 to F11 are closed.
3. **Closing them is the first brief of the third part,** to D5, before any dialer: each of F1 to F11 as listed here, closed as §1.9 says, with its test and its control, reviewed by D6 and D3. F11 is closed by keeping the link key out of the register (§1.1, §1.9 item 11), before any build makes a link key.
4. **No caller of `ServeSensor` outside the tests, and no caller of `sensorlink.Link.Serve` other than `ServeSensor`, merges before then.**

**Point 1 is a rule on people:** the binary still accepts the section, so until the couplings are closed the control is procedural, and the finding stays open as a release blocker (SL-20).

D3 records them in the threat model (AP-19, AP-34 (5), SL-20; HR-13 as the precedent), and D6 gives the set a finding number in `docs/verification.md`.

**Other facts this design rests on** (`desk`):

- `ServeSensor(ctx, io.Reader)` (`sensor.go`) takes no peer identity and cannot write back, and the node has one intake (`Runtime.sensor`). The link refuses a record under another `sensor_id` (`RefusedSensorID`) and a seq at or below the run's last (`Link.takeSeq`), and keeps its run and seq in memory only (`link.go`).
- `network_sensor` names one sensor (`NetworkSensor.SensorID`, `netsensor.go`), in one database, `network` (`NetworkDB`).
- No `sensor.coverage-gap` event is built: no file names it.
- The configuration is read at start only; `reload` reloads the suspensions and halted assets, nothing else (`reload.go`).
- The node does not apply `node.network_baseline_ack`: nothing under `platform/` or `cmd/` imports `modules/security/netack`, `relayKinds` does not list the kind (`adminrelay.go`), and no network learner runs on the node (§1.12).

## 1.1 The dialer and the firewall rule (brief item 1)

**Requirement, not built:** a new file `platform/sensorlink/dial.go`, with one dialer for each enrolled sensor. The sensor manager (§1.9) starts it after `Start` returns, never inside `Start`.

| Setting | Value | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Versions | `MinVersion` and `MaxVersion` both TLS 1.3 | SL-12 |
| Resumption | `SessionTicketsDisabled`, and no `ClientSessionCache`, so every handshake is a full one. Go's client sends no early data (an assumption, §1.14) | SL-12 |
| Peer check | **Normal verification is off, and `VerifyConnection` is the only check.** It refuses a resumed session (`DidResume`), a version other than TLS 1.3, and a connection whose ALPN was not negotiated; then it computes the leaf's pin and accepts only the pin of the enrolment in force at that moment, read through an atomic pointer that a revocation or a new enrolment replaces before it closes the link (§1.3). The configuration is built in one function, and a test shows that `InsecureSkipVerify` appears in that one product file only. D3 adds the matching line to HR-15's review rule (a client with no `RootCAs`) | SL-2, SL-12, SL-R2, HR-15 |
| Client certificate | `GetClientCertificate` returns a self-signed certificate made in memory at each node start, whose key is the node's link key. Nothing is written to disk. Its dates mean nothing: the node enforces the enrolment's `not_after` (SL-8) | SL-2 |
| ALPN | `otsec.netobs/0` (the major only) on both ends, refused if not negotiated (difference 8 on #627; CH-1) | SL-19 |
| Timeouts | TCP connect 5 s (node-local); C13 0.2's handshake 10 s, `sensor-hello` 10 s, idle 30 s (CH-4); a write deadline of 10 s for each node message (node-local); an `ack` at least every 10 s (CH-9) | SL-12, SL-20 |
| Back-off | 1 s, doubling to 60 s, with ±20 % jitter. It resets only after a connection whose hello was accepted and which lasted 60 s | SL-20 |
| Redial after `run-complete` | CH-38: **without back-off only when the node itself saw the run finished** (the agent closed cleanly **on a connection whose hello the node accepted**, and T had reached the run's `newest_seq` as the hello and ticks last stated it; D4's note 5 on #633), **and at least `RedialFloor` = 10 s has passed since the last such redial** (recorded ruling 2 (#628, comment 6803459738)), so at most 360 an hour. Otherwise the back-off applies. A clean close the node did not see as finished takes the back-off (D3's L1) | SL-20 |
| Connections | At most one for each sensor. The dialer is the only caller, and the intake refuses a second concurrent connection for the same sensor (`second-connection`) | SL-12 |
| Memory | One frame at a time, at most `MaxChannelFrame` (64 KiB) and its decoded record, as `Link.Serve` reads now; the node's own frames are under C13's 1 KiB (CH-2) | SL-20 |
| When it dials | Only while the configuration equals the live enrolment, the enrolment is neither revoked, fork-held, expired nor held by a pending revocation file (§1.3), the sensor's state is known, the link key is loaded (§1.9 item 11), **the sensor's database is open, its cursor is read and parses, and its intake has started with `Link.RecoverHold` done** (§1.4 item 5; CH-4, CH-18; D4's B5 on #633), **the address is no asset's**, and **the pair has a dial left under SL-30's limit** (both below). Otherwise it does not dial, and the alarm says why. **With the position unread** for any reason, `sensor-state-unread` stands with cause `position` (§1.3), beside `sensor-store-not-open` when the database is the reason and `sensor-intake-not-started` when the intake is (§1.9 items 1 and 2). *Why:* a node that dialled with its cursor unread would send no `taken`, read the counter as 0 at CH-13 row 3, save the agent's oldest run over the real cursor at row 6, and store that run's records a second time | SL-4, SL-9, SL-30, P1 |

- **The node never dials a device** (D3's L4, ruling 5). A sensor's IP address must be no IP address that any asset in the node's configuration has, in any endpoint or identity field. The comparison is on the IP alone, with any port, since a device's other ports are still the device. It is checked in four places:
  - **at load, and the reverse at load** (an asset added with an enrolled sensor's address): **never a refused configuration** (recorded ruling 1 (#628, comment 6803459738), D3's change 3). That sensor is not started and is never dialled, `sensor-address-is-asset` stands and names both, and collection for the asset goes on;
  - at enrolment: `sensor.enrolled` `denied`, reason `asset-address`;
  - at each dial, against the configuration as loaded;
  - in `otsec install-sensor-rules`, which refuses to write the rule.
  - Test: `TestSensorAddressNotAsset`, for each of the four places and the reverse, with the node started and a run stored in the two load cases; its control, the check removed, dials the simulator's device address and the test's listener sees the SYN.
- **The link key is in a file of its own, not in the node key register** (D3's NK-1 to NK-6, `docs/phase4/D3-sensor-link.md` §11.4; ruling 1 on #631 (comment 6804993239); this replaces "a kind of its own" in the register, which made F11):
  - **NK-1.** The file sits beside the register, under the same protected-file rule, and holds one kind of entry in the register's entry form: a reference to a key held by one of the register's non-exportable providers, its public key, its version and state, and who installed it and when. It holds no private key. The register keeps its two kinds, and a link-key entry put into the register is a register fault, as any unknown kind is today (`Entry.validate`).
  - **NK-2.** One reader returns the link key's signer and pin from the file, in a package that only `platform/sensorlink` imports (an import test). **Its one other input is the public keys of the register the node already loaded,** which the sensor manager passes in; it reads no other key. A link key whose public key is also a register entry is refused: the link is held back, and the register is not touched. **With `key_register` set and the register not loaded,** the clash cannot be checked, so the link is held back with the alarm while the node runs.
  - **NK-3.** The entry is made by the administrator's tool, never by the node at its start. It has no certificate chain and no pending state, because the link has no CA (SL-2). The handshake's self-signed certificate is made in memory at each start and never stored (above). The node writes `policy.apply` once for each state of the entry, as `RecordNodeKeys` does for a register entry (`platform/node/nodekeys.go`), and an unwritten record is an alarm.
  - **NK-4.** The pin of the key the handshake offers is in status, for the administrator to read (SL-5 step 2).
  - **NK-5.** The file is read by the sensor manager after `Start` returns, and any fault in it holds back the sensor links only (§1.9 item 11). An enrolment that arrives meanwhile stays pending (§1.3).
  - **NK-6.** No reader floor for the key register: its schema does not change (§1.15).
  - A local administrator can use the key (D3-7), and whoever can write the file can point the node at another key, which the sensor's end then refuses, failing closed. Replacing the key is SL-R8.
- **The dial limit before a sensor has answered** (SL-30; ruling 5 on #631 (comment 6804993239)). An enrolment can carry a mistyped address, and the node may not know the device that has it. So, for each pair of an enrolled address and pin, until the node has accepted a hello from that pin at that address, it makes **at most three dials**, and then stops dialling that sensor, with `sensor-dial-limit` standing, until an administrator acts. Afterwards the ordinary back-off and `RedialFloor` apply (CH-38).
  - **What a dial is:** every TCP connection the node begins towards the address, whatever becomes of it, **a refused one included,** counted as one however many SYN segments the operating system sends inside it. It is abandoned at the node's 5 s connect timeout, which is this dialer's own figure and not a new one.
  - **The count and the proof are in `sensorrun.json`,** keyed by the address and the pin, and **saved and synced before each dial. A save that fails forbids the dial,** with the alarm. **A count that cannot be read is taken as spent,** so a node whose state cannot be read or written dials nothing to a pair that has not answered.
  - **A run file that is behind reads as unreadable** (SL-30 as #641 amends it; D4's B2 on #633; D1's alarm half in D4-90). A rename lost at a power cut can leave `sensorrun.json` readable and older than what the node last synced, because the folder flush does nothing on Windows (`platform/store/os_windows.go` `syncDir`; D4-65). **D5's choice of SL-30's two means is the write number:**
    - `sensorrun.json` carries `write_no`, one above the last, **starting at 1; 0 means that no file has been written. A new database's first cursor carries the run file's current `write_no`, or 0 when no file has been written yet** (§1.3, enrolment step 3), so a database created at commissioning, before the first dial's save, is current with the file, and makes no file read as behind or lost. **A database that holds no write number (no cursor) while the run file holds one reads as lost** (SL-30 at `41a073f2`; D3's rule 2, posted first as comment 6838033945 on #641), as an absent one does; that covers a creation interrupted before its first commit. **A first cursor of 0 is a number, not its absence:** a database created at commissioning before any file carries it, and a crash after the file's first write and before the cursor's commit leaves the file at 1 above it, which is current, as every file is between its write and that commit; reading 0 as none would take that crash for a loss and cost a re-arm. A re-created database never carries 0, since rule 2 writes the file first. *Test:* `TestRunFileFirstWriteKill`, SL-T23 (r) at #641's `9d74bb73` (D3's ruling (a), comment 6839793790 on #644), on a node with no run file and a `new` enrolment of a pair that has never answered: the node restarted before any dial, three dials and then none; killed after the first dial's file write and before the cursor's commit, the next start reads the file as current and the count as one, and makes two more dials and then none; and the enrolment's record made to fail after the database's creation, then the node restarted, the enrolment is not live, and once it is applied again, three dials (*controls:* a node that reads a cursor of 0 as no write number, which holds the pair spent after the kill and dials nothing; and a node that creates the database only at the first dial, which reads it as absent at the first restart and dials nothing; **for the third case** (D6's N2 on #641, SL-T23 (r) at `467c1dd6`), a node that takes a database's existence for a live enrolment, which dials before the enrolment is applied again, and a node that reads the database left by the failed enrolment as a lost one, which holds the pair spent once it is applied again and dials nothing). After the file is written (temporary file, flush, rename), the node saves the same number in the cursor of every open sensor database, with `SaveSourceCursor` (§1.4), **and always in the database of the sensor being dialled** (SL-30 at `467c1dd6`; D6's N5 on #641), so the check that a file is behind never rests only on another sensor's database for a pair this sensor dials. A save that SL-30 requires before a dial is complete only once that commit, in the dialled sensor's database, has returned; a dial to a sensor whose database is not open waits, as the dial condition already requires it open.
    - At a read, a file whose `write_no` is below the highest number in any sensor database is **behind**; a file that is missing while a sensor database holds a number above 0 is **lost**. Both read as unreadable: the count is spent unless a cursor of the live generation proves the pair. A file missing while no database holds a number above 0 (a 0 says that no file had been written), and no live enrolment's database is missing or without a cursor (below), is a new node's, and reads as empty.
    - The node cannot compare the file with a sensor database it cannot read. The counts of the pairs of that database's sensor then read as spent. A cursor that holds a write number and no run is not a position, and proves nothing under SL-30.
    - **A sensor database missing under a live enrolment reads, for SL-30, as lost, whatever the run file holds** (D6's B2 on #641, comment 6837825426, which D3 adopts), **and so does one with no cursor,** since enrolment step 3 commits the first cursor before the record that makes the enrolment live: the count of each pair of that sensor reads as spent, no cursor proves the pair, and the next dial waits for an administrator's re-arm, with `sensor-dial-limit` standing. Without this, a pair that never answered, two dials, and a power cut that loses both the database's creation and the run file's last rename would read as a new node's and get three more dials, five in all; with the file behind instead (its count at one), two more, four in all; with the file kept, one more, three in all, which SL-30 allows but which still dials past the re-arm the rule asks for. **So that a missing database is a lost one,** the manager creates the sensor's database, for a sensor that has none, and commits its first cursor, with the run file's current `write_no` (0 when none has been written), **before it writes the succeeded `sensor.enrolled`** (§1.3, enrolment step 3; SL-30 at `41a073f2`), and so before `sensors.json` and before any dial. A live enrolment has then always had a database. A crash between the creation and the record leaves the request pending with its database, and costs nothing. The one case that still reads as lost without a loss is a power cut that undoes the creation's rename (`syncDir` does nothing on Windows) while the audit record, committed after it, survives; it fails closed, and costs a re-arm.
    - **A run file that cannot be compared with a sensor's database reads as lost for every value it holds for that sensor** (SL-30 at #641's `467c1dd6`; D6's B4 on #641; D1's alarm half in D4-90). "Cannot be compared" means that, under a live enrolment, the sensor's database is lost, absent, unread or without a cursor. "Behind" is otherwise judged against the other databases only, and on a node with one sensor against none, so a stale file would read as current. So the file's values for that sensor, and for every pair that sensor's enrolments name, read as lost, as SL-30's counts already do: the counts spent, the standing `sensor-transmitted` raised until an administrator clears it (§1.8), the poison record, the cap's last record times and held pool events read as possibly lost (§1.9 items 5, 6 and 9), and the re-arm's spent set rebuilt. **Two exceptions,** each of which only moves something earlier or is checked again: the copy of the earlier of W and R, which serves only as a bound that a stale copy moves earlier (§1.9 item 5), and the run copy that `node-hello` names after a lost database (SL-31, §1.4), whose cost is stated there. `sensor-state-unread` stands with cause `run-file`. *Test:* SL-T24 (g)'s third and fourth runs, in `TestRunFileRolledBack` (§1.4).
    - **"Spent" holds across any number of restarts until a re-arm** (SL-30 at `41a073f2`; D3's rule 2, posted first as comment 6838033945 on #641). When the node reads a sensor's counts as spent because its database is lost, it does three things in this order:
      1. it writes the run file first, with every pair of that sensor marked spent (its count at three and its proof cleared), under a new `write_no`, synced;
      2. only then does it create the database again, and the database's first commit carries that `write_no`;
      3. at every later start it finds one of three things: the spent marks; a file behind the database, which reads as unreadable and so as spent; or a database lost again, or holding no write number, which reads as lost and so as spent.

      Only a re-arm's grant clears a spent mark. A later cursor of the live generation still proves the pair once a hello is accepted, but no hello comes before a re-arm.
    - Test: SL-T23 (p) (D6's B2 on #641), `TestDialLimitLostDatabase`: after two dials to a pair that has never answered, the node stopped and the sensor database deleted, three times over: with `sensorrun.json` kept, with it replaced by its copy from before the second dial, and with it deleted too. In each case no source port, and the alarm stands. *Control:* a node that reads a missing database as a new node's and then takes the file as it finds it, which makes one more dial in the first case, two in the second and three in the third. **SL-T23 (q)** (D3's rule 2), `TestDialLimitSpentAcrossRestarts`: after (p)'s first case, the node restarted twice, with the run file replaced before each start by its copy from before the second dial, and in one run the node killed between the database's re-creation and its first commit: no source port at any start until a re-arm. *Controls:* a node that marks nothing before it creates the database again, which dials after the second start; and a node that trusts the file when the database holds no write number, which dials after the kill.
    - It costs one more FULL commit for each write of the file, which is already bounded (a new run per connection under `RedialFloor`, three dials a pair, the cap's records once an hour, held events at most once a minute), and a dial waits for that commit. No contract changes: the file, the cursor's form and `SaveSourceCursor` are the node's own (§1.4).
    - Test: SL-T23 (o), which for this node expects no further source port after the file is replaced by an older copy or deleted (control: a node that trusts a readable file, which sends two more; and one that reads a missing file as a new node's, which sends three more).
  - **The one proof that does not need the file** is the sensor database's cursor, which an accepted hello leaves (§1.4). **Only a cursor of the live enrolment's generation proves that enrolment's pair:** the cursor names a generation and a run, and no address or pin, so a cursor of an earlier generation, a `renew` of the same pair included **until a hello of that generation has been accepted and saved (CH-13 rows 5b and 6)**, or of a revoked generation, proves nothing, and the count reads as spent.
  - **The proof is saved before the hello that earns it is accepted:** for a new run, with the new run, in the same write as `prior` (CH-13 row 6); **for a `renew`'s first resume under the new generation, by CH-13 row 5b's save of that generation in the cursor** (§1.4; ruling 1 on #633 and #634 (comment 6805936000)). Until it is saved, a lost state file means the count reads as spent. So a restart, a crash, an edited entry that still equals the enrolment, an unreadable state directory, a run file that is behind, and anything a peer does never give a fourth dial. **"Before it is accepted" means before CH-13 row 7:** rows 5b and 6 save before row 7's `pinned_by` check, so a hello then closed as `binding` has saved the proof. The handshake had already shown the pinned key at the enrolled address, so SL-30 is not weakened (D4's wording note on #633).
  - **The count belongs to the pair,** not to the configuration entry or the `sensor_id`, and outlives both. It is dropped only when no enrolment in the chain names the pair that a `renew` could follow: a live, a pending or an expired one. A `renew` keeps the pair and its proof; a `rekey`, a `replace` or another address is a new pair and starts at three.
  - **The re-arm.** An elevated subcommand, `otsec sensor-redial -address <ip> -pin <pin>`, writes one request file into `admin_dir` under the protected-file rule, carrying its own time and the person it claims. The sensor manager grants three dials only if the address and pin equal the live enrolment. Before the grant it records the file's hash as spent in `sensorrun.json`, and writes a `sensor.redial-granted` health event that names the hash and the claimed person. **When the sensor's health-event cap is spent, that record is held in `sensorrun.json` as any held event is (§1.9 item 5), and the first dial waits until it is durable, written or held.** Then the file is removed. **A request file whose own time is before the time the spent set was last rebuilt is refused,** so a file put back after `sensorrun.json` was lost cannot grant three more under an old act's name. **A run file that is behind or lost, or unreadable, rebuilds the spent set** (SL-30 as #641 amends it): the set starts empty and its rebuild time is that start's, so every request file written before it is refused. **This guards the record's truth, not a privilege** (D3): an administrator writes the file's time, and whoever can write the file can make a new act anyway. This meets SL-30's four conditions: a person's act at the node, never a restart or timer; the address and pin named and checked against a live enrolment; three dials per act, spent durably before the first; a durable record before the first dial. It needs no C7 kind and no C8 action, and it writes no audit record (C8-7).
  - **Residuals** (SL-30): up to three attempts of at most 5 s each to a device at a mistyped address, on whatever port the enrolment names; and an address that once answered is redialled at the ordinary back-off without a limit.
- **`ServeSensor` changes shape** (CH-25, D5-12): `ServeSensor(ctx, peer sensorlink.Peer, conn io.ReadWriter)`. **`sensorlink.Peer` can be made only from a finished handshake:** its fields are unexported and its one constructor takes a `tls.ConnectionState` whose pin the enrolment accepted, so no later caller can hand `ServeSensor` an identity it invented (D3's condition). The link that serves it is chosen from `peer`, never from a record (SL-10). D6's three tests that call today's form move with the third part.
- **The node's close reasons that this design adds or causes,** from C13's closed set (CH-11 has fifteen; difference 7 on #627; CH-37): `store` (§1.4), `position-not-saved` (§1.4), `second-connection`, `enrolment` for a revocation, the revocation fast path or **any enrolment of the sensor, a `renew` with the pin unchanged included** (§1.3; SL-R6; D4's B1 on #633, adopted in D4-90), `shutdown` when the node stops (CH-11, CH-37), and `state-unread`, in CH-11, when a record in the sensor fold cannot be parsed (§1.3). **An edited `network_sensor` entry closes as `shutdown`, not `enrolment`:** the configuration is read at start only, so the open connection ends because the node stops, and the restarted node does not dial because the entry no longer equals the enrolment (SL-9; CH-37). After an `enrolment` decision the node takes no further record of the connection and sends no `ack`, and the close is a TLS close with no message (CH-37).
- **The counts.** The node builds C13's `link_closes` and `dial_failures` (C13 §0's table, CH-1, CH-11) in status. **A connection the agent closes after the handshake and before the node has read a `sensor-hello`** (its `binding` after a `renew`, for one) is counted under `link_closes` as `closed-before-hello`, the name D4 adds to CH-11's counts (D4's note 5 on #633). It is not `hello-timeout`, and it is never `run-complete` (the redial row above).
- **The firewall rule.** `platform/winsvc` `Install` adds one rule today, which blocks the node service's outbound TCP to port 23 (`install_windows.go`). **Requirement:** a subcommand `otsec install-sensor-rules -config <file>` in `cmd/otsec`, which an administrator runs elevated after each enrolment. It adds, for each configured sensor, a rule named `OTSEC node to sensor <sensor_id>`: outbound, allow, service `otsec-node`, TCP, that sensor's address and port, and refuses an address that is an asset's. The node never changes a firewall rule itself (CLAUDE.md §10). A block rule still wins over it (D3). **Assumption, not read:** that Windows' default outbound policy is allow, so the rule matters only where a site blocks outbound by default. The guard on every site is that the node dials only the enrolled address. No inbound rule is added and no listener is opened (SL-18).
- **Tests.** In `platform/sensorlink`, over loopback TLS with test keys: `TestDialRefusesTLS12`; `TestDialNoResumption`, whose control is a client session cache, which makes the second connection resume; `TestDialRefusesOtherPin`, whose control is a `VerifyConnection` that accepts any key; `TestDialPinReadAtHandshake`; `TestDialRefusesNoALPN`; `TestDialHandshakeStall`; `TestDialBackoff` on the test clock; `TestRedialAfterRunComplete`, whose control redials at once after any clean close, and its leg for `RedialFloor`; `TestOneConnectionPerSensor`; `TestInsecureSkipVerifyOneFile`; the import test for the link key's reader (NK-2); and SL-30's `TestDialLimit`, SL-T23's legs (a) to (q) on the test clock and the rig, counted by source port, each with its control. Leg (m): with a cursor of an earlier or a revoked generation, and the count unreadable, the node makes no dial and `sensor-dial-limit` stands; its control takes any cursor as proof, and dials. Leg (m) also takes H-7's case: after a `renew`'s first resume is saved, a lost count reads as proven. Leg (o), a run file that is behind, is above. `TestNoDialWithoutPosition` (D4's B5): with the sensor database open and its cursor's read made to fail, and again with the intake not started, no SYN goes to the sensor and `sensor-state-unread` stands with cause `position`; control: a node that dials with the cursor unread, which saves the agent's oldest run over the real cursor and stores its records a second time. On the rig: SL-T3, SL-T7, SL-T8, SL-T11, and SL-T13's leg (h) (§1.9). The firewall rule is read back in the dispatched Windows workflow (`a5-windows-experiments.yml`) where a hosted runner allows it, and is `unknown` where it doesn't.

## 1.2 The `network_sensor` configuration, and one sensor or two (brief item 2)

**Requirement, not built.** The section keeps its node-wide fields (`evidence_class`, `outage_hours`, `alert_retention_days`, `node_addresses`, `node_macs`, `node_vlans`) and gains:

- `link_key_ref`: the node's link key, an entry of the link-key file that sits beside the key register (§1.1, NK-1), not an entry of the register. One key for the node, as SL-R8 assumes.
- `sensors`: a list, each entry with `sensor_id`, `address` (an IP literal) and `port`, `sensor_spki` (the pin) and `config_digest` (D5-4, §1.15). The pin is the **trust anchor**, since there is no CA (SL-2). The link key is the **credential**. The **permitted peer** is the `sensor_id`, address and pin together, which must equal the live enrolment (SL-9).
- `retired`: `sensor_id`s whose streams are kept until drained and pruned (§1.7), at most `MaxSensors`. **Why that number:** a retired database costs its quotas in the volume rule and a share of the platform ceiling (§1.9), and a node only ever retires what it ran.
- `blind_after_s` (§1.8), `late_bound_s` (CH-23, **180 s** by default, 30 s to 3,600 s, recorded ruling 2 (#628, comment 6803459738)) and `backlog_tie_s` (CH-39, 60 s by default, at most 120 s, recorded ruling 2 (#628, comment 6803459738); §1.6).

`max_skew_ms` (§1.5, CH-22), 2,000 ms, is the node's fixed bound and not a field of the section (D4's note 6 on #633).

**One sensor or two (ruling 1).** The list holds at most `MaxSensors` = 2. **The third part builds one:** a second entry is not started, and `sensor-not-built` stands, until a second is built; the node starts and collects as without it. A second waits for the owner's answer on PRP (O-12), and nothing that merges two sensors' records is designed before it. What a second would cost, for that decision:

| | One sensor (built in the third part) | Two sensors (later, on O-12) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Intake | One link and one hold | A link and a hold for each. `dispatchHold`, `resultHold` and `abandonHold` call each sensor's link. `collection.run` carries `after_seq.<sensor_id>` and `open_flow.<sensor_id>`, and `runSessionOf` reads its own sensor's keys, and the plain keys of a record from before as the single sensor's. The handover barrier (`intakeRecords`) is kept for each sensor |
| Store | One database, `network-<sensor_id>` | A database for each, so a quota, which is per class in its database, keeps one sensor's flood from dropping the other's records. A second set of network quotas in the volume rule |
| Baseline and detector | One of each | One of each for each sensor, on its own `netbaseline/<sensor_id>` stream. A person signs an acknowledgement for each. On PRP, traffic on both LANs alerts twice (CH-30 option (ii)) |

**The database's name.** It is `network-<sensor_id>` from the third part on. A `network` database left by a node that ran the merged pipe-era intake stays on disk unopened and is never deleted, as every store file is (Part 2 §5). No site has one: `evidence_class` must be `simulated` (`checkNetworkSensor`). The places that test for `NetworkDB` change (`commitcap.go`, `runtime.go` `startDB`'s `WallMark`, `netretention.go`, `sensor.go`, `netdetector.go`), and the sensor databases move out of `Runtime.dbs` (§1.9 item 1).

**No sensor check fails the node's start or refuses its configuration, except the section's own shape** (recorded ruling 1 (#628, comment 6803459738), D3 and D6). The shape checks stay load failures, as SL-9 says: the top-level `sensor_id` (refused with a message that names `sensors`), a third entry, a duplicate ID, address or pin, an address that isn't an IP literal, and an ID both in `sensors` and in `retired`. **Everything else that concerns a sensor leaves the node to start and collect, with that sensor not started and an alarm standing:** an address that is an asset's, or an asset with an enrolled sensor's address (§1.1); the platform floor (§1.9 item 5); a second entry before a second is built; and a mismatch with the enrolment (§1.3). A sensor's paperwork or sizing never stops SER or Syslog collection (SL-9, SL-20). Code: `NetworkSensor` and `checkNetworkSensor` (`netsensor.go`), `networkLayout`, `Config.Consumers` (`layout.go`), and every reader of `NetworkSensor.SensorID`.

**Tests**, in the new package of §1.9: `TestNetworkSensorsShape` (none, three, a duplicate and the old top-level `sensor_id` refused at load; two while one is built, and an asset's address, each starting the node with that sensor not started and its alarm, whose control is a load refusal), and for the later second sensor `TestTwoSensorsSeparateDatabases` (A's observation class filled, B's records still commit; control: one shared database, where B's drop), `TestCollectionRunAfterSeqPerSensor` and `TestRunSessionReadsPlainAfterSeq`.

## 1.3 Enrolment, revocation and expiry (brief item 3)

### Where the state lives

D3 asks that the live enrolments and the revoked pins never depend on the audit stream alone, which is pruned (SL-6). On `main` the suspensions are kept as a checkpoint file, `suspensions.json` in the store directory, folded from the audit stream, with the C5 consumer `node-suspensions` holding the stream's prune point (`suspend.go`, `loadSuspensions`, `pruneConsumers`). This design follows that pattern and needs no C5 change, with D3's conditions:

- **`sensors.json`**, schema `otsec.node-sensors/0.1`, written whole to a temporary file and renamed. It holds `through_seq`; for each `sensor_id` its generation, pin, address, the node's pin, `not_after`, the decision's `decision_seq`, payload hash and record time (`enrolled_at`, the earliest bound for a coverage gap whenever the node holds no W for the sensor, its database lost or the sensor never watched; §1.9 item 5), its state (`live`, `revoked`, `fork-held`), the first `pinned_by` of the generation (CH-33), and **the generation at which the node's position began** (SL-R6 as #641 amends it; below); the revoked pins, never removed; and the revocation records owed. It also keeps `applied_at` with each owed `sensor.revoked` (§1.3, "Revocation"). **Nothing that changes at the sensor's will is in it** (D3's preference): the second copy of the run counter, refusal times, SL-30's dial counts and proofs, the re-arm's spent request hashes, the held events of the pool, the poison record, the standing `sensor-transmitted`, and a copy of the earlier of W and R (§1.9 item 5) are in a second file, `sensorrun.json`, under the write number (§1.1, §1.4, §1.9 items 5, 6 and 9), so a reconnect loop never rewrites the file that holds the pins. **What changes with a run is in the sensor database's cursor** (§1.4): the position, the run's spans, `prior`, the run's `config_digest`, the heartbeat baselines, and W with what is kept with it: R and, from W's heartbeat, each interface's `packets` and the `at_utc` of its last rise in the run (§1.9 item 5). `sensorrun.json` no longer holds spans, `prior` or the digest (SL-31; D3's preference on #634).
- **A consumer `node-sensors`** on the audit stream, added by `pruneConsumers` beside `node-suspensions` and `node-holds`. Its cursor is kept **one write behind the file**: after a new file is written, the cursor moves to the previous file's `through_seq`, so a file that a power cut rolls back still finds its records (D4-65's open question about a rename's durability does not wait).
- **A rolled-back or old file** (D3's condition 1): a file whose `through_seq` is below the cursor is folded forward only if every audit record after it is still in the stream. A gap makes the state unknown, and nothing dials. A revoked pin therefore never comes back live from an old file.
- **Its own fold.** `foldSensors`, in a new file `platform/node/sensorstate.go`, reads the audit stream from the file's `through_seq` in pages of 64, each read under a 1 s deadline, yielding between pages, and runs in the sensor manager, never in the scheduler's `fold`. A sensor record that doesn't parse makes only the sensor's state unknown: **every open sensor connection is closed and nothing dials,** `sensor-state-unread` is raised, and collection goes on (the rule below). A fold that fails to read leaves the state unread, under the read-error rules below. **One alarm name for state or a position that cannot be read or cannot be parsed** (D1 on #633, after `1ef65291`; ruling 4 on #633 and #634 (comment 6805936000)). The reason is in `loadSuspensions`: an error in the suspension fold holds every run (`asset-suspensions-unknown`). **The one name covers four causes that act differently, so status and its event carry the cause as a detail, `cause`** (D4's note on #633; D5's N3 on #634):

  | `cause` | What cannot be read | What the node does |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `fold-record` | A record in the sensor fold that cannot be parsed | Closes every open sensor connection (`state-unread`) and dials none (below) |
  | `fold-read` | The fold's read failed (a deadline, the database busy or not open) | Keeps the links this run has confirmed by a full fold, dials no other, and retries (below) |
  | `position` | A sensor database's cursor, or the database itself (§1.1's dial row; CH-4, CH-18) | Does not dial that sensor |
  | `run-file` | `sensorrun.json` unreadable, lost or behind, or not comparable with a sensor's database (lost, absent, unread or without a cursor), which reads as lost for every value of that sensor (§1.1) | Dials on the cursor's proof; the count is spent otherwise; `sensor-transmitted` is raised (§1.8) |
- **A read error and a bad record are not the same** (D3's change 2 on `4d03075a`, keeping `main`'s distinction, `badRecord` in `suspend.go`):
  - **A read error** (a deadline passed, the database busy or not open) is retried at the next manager tick **with the cursor held**. The state is `sensor-state-unread` meanwhile, and the revoked pins stay in force. Two rules keep it from failing open (D3 on `e2ff17c7`; SL-R2, P5):
    - **After a start, nothing dials until one fold has read through the audit stream's head.** Before that the state in memory is the file's, which may predate a revocation whose file write failed. A read error later in the run keeps only the links this run has already confirmed by such a fold.
    - **A read error never delays a revocation.** An accepted `node.sensor_revoke` that the scheduler hands over is applied in memory at once, whatever the fold's state, and the fast path's hold stays until it is.
    - Test: `TestSensorUnreadAtStartDialsNothing` (a failed revocation write, a restart, and a first read that fails: nothing is dialled; control: the node dials under the file).
  - **A record that cannot be parsed** makes the state unknown. **While a record in the fold cannot be parsed, the node closes every open sensor connection and dials none, a live one included** (D1's ruling on #633, "D1's ruling on an open connection"), until the record is read or an administrator acts, with `sensor-state-unread` standing, the one name, whose event is in the pool (§1.9 item 5). The record may be a revocation, so a sensor that kept its link or kept dialling could be a revoked one (D3 on #633 at `32662d3a`; fail closed). **The close reason** is **`state-unread`** (CH-11): `enrolment` is a decision the node has applied (CH-37), and `store` is a store error that leaves the records with the sensor; here no decision is known. The node takes no further record of the connection, sends no `ack`, and closes with TLS `close_notify` and no C13 message, as CH-37's first two steps do. The records already read are finished as at any close, and T does not move past what is durable (§1.4). It writes no audit record (C8-7).
  - **The cursor moves on** past what the fold could not use only for such a record, or when the audit class holds more than three quarters of its quota, so the cursor never holds the audit class past that fill (F8). **Before it moves, the manager writes to `sensors.json` every revoked pin it has read,** and the file's revoked pins stay in force through the unknown state and the next enrolment. So a slow read never loses an enrolment or a revocation (SL-6), and a bad record loses at most the enrolments after it, never a revocation already read.
  - Nothing dials after an unknown state until the record is read or an administrator acts, by a new enrolment, which the fold then reads from its own record. The cursor is saved at most once a minute, outside any loop over records.
  - **What the field recovery costs** (D5's N10 on #634). If the record becomes readable, the link resumes from `taken` with the sensor's buffer intact (C13's M-10). If it does not, the state stays unknown, and only a `rekey` or a `replace` is accepted (below): each needs the sensor's console, starts the node's position again, and has the sensor discard its buffer, declared in `prior`. The interval is a coverage gap, cause `held` (§1.9 item 5).
  - Test: `TestSensorStateReadErrorRetried` (a read that fails once: the state returns at the next tick and the stream is intact; control: the cursor moved on at once, which loses the enrolment once the stream is pruned).
- **A failed write of `fork-held`** fails closed like a failed revocation write: the dialer stays off for the run, and the fold derives the hold from `sensor.link_refused` (`run-forked`) at the next start (D3's condition 3).
- **With the file lost and the stream pruned past what it needs,** the state is unknown and nothing dials. **Only a `rekey` or a `replace` is then accepted** (C7-4; ruling 4 on #631 (comment 6804993239)), at a generation above every generation of that `sensor_id` still in the chain, with no pin comparison, and `sensor.enrolled` carries `previous_state: unknown` (C8-2). **A `new` and a `renew` are `denied` (`reason`):** the node cannot show that no enrolment exists, or that the pin is unchanged. A new node is not in that state: with no state file and a chain kept from its start, its state is known and empty, and `new` works. **Limit:** a revoked pin that only the lost file held could be enrolled again by a new signature.

### Enrolment

`relayKinds` and `nodeActKinds` gain `node.sensor_enrol` and `node.sensor_revoke` (`adminrelay.go`), and `applyAdminPart` gains two cases, bound to the install key as `node.baseline_ack` is (`admin_actions.go`). **Each case only hands the decision to the sensor manager over a buffered channel and returns** (D3's note 3): the part shows `pending` until the manager reports it done, so the scheduler never waits on the manager. The manager:

1. writes `policy.apply` with `admin_keys` first when the acceptance names a new key set (C8 0.12's rule, `netack.NeedsGrantRecord`);
2. makes C8-2's seven checks, **in this order, so that one request has one reason:** (1) `node-key`, the node's pin is its link key's; (2) `generation`, one above the last, or 1, or with the state unknown above every generation still in the chain; (3) `revoked-key`, the pin is not one the node has revoked, under any `sensor_id`; (4) `forked-key`, nor one it holds for a fork; (5) **`reason`, the reason agrees with the pin by C7-4's table** (`renew` with the same pin, `rekey` and `replace` with another, `new` with no enrolment), and is neither a `new` nor a `renew` while the state is unknown; (6) `not-after`, `not_after` is after `issued_at` and at most 366 days after it; (7) `asset-address`, the address is no asset's (§1.1). A failed check writes `sensor.enrolled` with outcome `denied` and that reason, and changes nothing. **A denial is final, so a fault of the node's own denies nothing** (C8-2; ruling 4 on #631 (comment 6804993239)): check 1 is made only while the link key is loaded, and checks 2 to 5 only while the sensor state has been read. **With the link key not loaded, or the state unread, the request stays pending**, with an alarm, and is tried again; no `sensor.enrolled` is written;
3. **when the checks pass and the sensor has no database yet, creates the sensor's database and commits its first cursor, with the run file's current `write_no` (0 when none has been written; §1.1), before it writes the succeeded `sensor.enrolled`** (SL-30 at `41a073f2`; D6's B2 on #641). A live enrolment has then always had a database, so one absent under a live enrolment is a loss, or a power cut close to the enrolment, and both read as lost (§1.1). A creation that fails leaves the part pending, with the alarm, and writes no record; a database created for a request whose audit write then fails stays unused until the request is tried again. A denied request creates nothing;
4. writes `sensor.enrolled` with `c5.AuditBeforeAct`, and applies nothing if that write fails (the part stays pending and is tried again);
5. **closes that sensor's open connection, if any, with `enrolment`, whatever the enrolment's reason, a `renew` with the pin unchanged included** (D3's L3 (iii); SL-R6; D4's B1 on #633, adopted in D4-90 and confirmed by D3 in #641), so the old pin is replaced at once (SL-6). One rule, with no branch on the reason. Its cost: after a `renew`, the node's redials end at the agent's `binding` until the sensor's end holds the renewal (SL-5 step 6); the pair keeps its proof (SL-30), and the interval is a coverage gap (§1.9 item 5);
6. applies it in memory, writes `sensors.json`, and starts the dialer if the configuration matches. A mismatch raises `sensor-not-enrolled` and dials nothing. **An enrolment that is live but not configured** shows in status (D3's note 5).

**What a new enrolment does to the position** (CH-12; ruling 5 on #627 with D3's condition). It depends on the signed `reason` **and on whether the sensor's pin is unchanged**, never on the reason alone; D3 adds to C7 0.11 that `renew` needs the same pin and `rekey` and `replace` a different one.

- **`renew` with the pin unchanged:** the generation rises, and **the node's saved position carries over** (the cursor, §1.4, and the run's second copy in `sensorrun.json`), as the agent's buffer does. So an expired enrolment, renewed, loses nothing, and the sensor's old runs are not closed as `run-regressed`.
- **Any other enrolment (`new`, `rekey`, `replace`):** run 1 of the new generation begins. The node's saved position reads as none from that generation on (below): `node-hello` carries no `taken`, CH-13 row 3 reads the saved counter as 0, and the first hello is a new run (SL-16; CH-6, CH-13; D4's note 3 on #633).
- **The generation at which the position began** (SL-R6 as #641 amends it; D5's B4 on #634). `sensors.json` keeps it for each `sensor_id`: a `new`, a `rekey` and a `replace` set it to their generation, and a `renew` with the pin unchanged keeps it. The fold derives it from `sensor.enrolled`'s `generation`, `sensor_spki`, `reason` and `previous_spki` (C8-2), so a lost `sensors.json` still yields it while the chain holds those records; with the state unknown only a `rekey` or a `replace` is accepted (below), and either sets it. **A saved position whose generation is below it reads as none.** So `node-hello` carries no `taken`, CH-13 row 3 reads the saved counter as 0, and the agent's run arrives as a new run (CH-6, CH-13; D4's note 3 on #633). A `taken` from the old generation would make the agent's `runs_skipped` fall below zero. *Why:* a `rekey` under which no hello is accepted, then a `renew`, would otherwise carry the older key's position over: the first hello closes as `run-regressed`, writes a false replay record, and the new pair never earns SL-30's proof, so the link stops at the dial limit.
  - **It is never done by writing a reset cursor at the enrolment** (D4-90): under SL-30 a cursor of the live generation proves the pair, and that pair has not answered.
  - **A position that reads as none, or that row 6's save replaces, does not drop what it still owes** (SL-31; D5's N3 on #641). Before the cursor is replaced, the node writes every `lost` gap still marked owed and the `sensor.run-loss` of a `prior` still marked owed, or carries those marks into the new cursor. A kill in between gives them twice, never not at all.
- **On `replace`,** the old host may never send the hello that would declare its discarded tail, so the node writes its own count of the old generation's unsent tail, the last `newest_seq` that host stated less T, in a `sensor.run-loss` event. That `newest_seq` is kept in the cursor (§1.4), so the count survives a node restart since the last connection (D5's N8 on #634).

### Revocation

Once the collector's acceptance is folded:

**A verified revocation is never dropped** (C8-3; ruling 3 on #631 (comment 6804993239)). **Its outcome is always `succeeded`, and it always adds its pin to the node's revoked pins, under any `sensor_id`,** whatever the node holds: an enrolment still pending (which is then `denied`, `revoked-key`, at its turn); the pin of an older generation (the live generation's link is untouched, and a later enrolment naming the old pin is `denied`); and a node whose sensor state is unknown or unread (the pin is in force from the acceptance on, in memory, before the state is read, and stays through that state). **If no link used that pin, `streams` is present and empty.** A second revocation of a pin already revoked is recorded too, with an empty `streams`. **No state of the node holds a revocation up, and nothing lifts it** (D5's N1 on #631; SL-R2; ruling 2 on #633 and #634 (comment 6805936000)). `applyAdminPart` binds `node.sensor_enrol` to the install key, as `node.baseline_ack` is. A revocation can only stop a link, so while a collector reset is open, or for a retired install key, **the pin is added to the revoked pins at once, in memory and in `sensors.json`, as for any verified revocation, and the link is closed at once.** This is not the fast path's hold, whose lifting rules (a refusal, the file's removal, the request's expiry) do not apply: nothing lifts a verified revocation. **Only the `sensor.revoked` record waits,** owed with its `applied_at`, durable in `sensors.json`, until the install key is trusted again, as `sensor.revoked` may already be written late.

1. mark the pin revoked in memory, with **`applied_at`, the node's time of that mark** (C8-3), and write `sensors.json`;
2. close the connection with `enrolment` and stop the dialer. A dial in its handshake fails at `VerifyConnection`. **Steps 3 to 5 begin only after the read loop has returned** (`Link.Serve` and `ServeSensor` return), so no record is being committed (D3's L3 (i));
3. release every run that sensor's hold still holds, tied where its result came and untied otherwise, each in one release (`Link.release`), so no held record is left;
4. take the observation stream's `through_seq` as its last committed seq. **The alert stream's `through_seq` is taken once the detector has judged every observation at or below the observation stream's `through_seq`** (D3's L3 (ii)), so no alert from the sensor's earlier observations lands above it unmarked;
5. write C8 `sensor.revoked`, with both `through_seq` values (or an empty `streams` when no link was ended) and `applied_at`. Until step 4's alert half is done, the record is owed, **and `applied_at` is kept with the owed record in `sensors.json`,** because it may be written after a restart; `applied_at` is never after the record's own time;
6. commit a health event **`sensor.revocation-applied`** to the health stream, not to the alert stream, under a name of its own so that it does not share the audit action's (SL-R3; ruling 4 on #631 (comment 6804993239)).

- **From the fold of the acceptance to the close: within 1 s** (ruling 2), tested by `TestSensorRevokeCloseWithinOneSecond` on the test clock; its control takes the close after the audit write, which a failing write then holds open.
- If step 1 fails, the dialer stays off for the rest of the run and `sensor-state-not-durable` is raised. At the next start the fold derives the revocation from the decision in the chain: **`relayRecord` (`adminrelay.go`) writes the sensor's fields on the relayed `collector.request` only when the outcome is `allowed` and the record carries `verified`,** as `Target.sensor_id` and a `sensor` object with the verified body's `generation` and `sensor_spki`, and for a revocation its `reason` and `suspect_from`; never as text in `detail`, and never on an `info` or `denied` record (C8-6).
- If step 5 fails, the link stays closed, the record is owed in `sensors.json` and in status, and the manager writes it once when the audit chain takes writes again.
- **How soon "at once" is (ruling 2, option (a)).** `relayAdmin` runs between runs, never during one, every `RelayEverySeconds` (15 s), and a run lasts at most `MaxDeadlineMS` (600 s) (`adminrelay.go`, `schedule.go` `Run`). So, from signing to the close, about 15 s when no run is in progress, and about 615 s at worst **when the collector answers. While `collectorHold` holds requests, `relayAdmin` sends nothing, and the bound is open.** The collector's pipe takes no second client (D3: a change to the privileged process that this gain does not justify). **Why minutes are tolerable:** every record committed in the window is at or below `through_seq` and after `suspect_from`, so every reader shows it as from a revoked sensor (SL-R3).
- **The fast path (ruling 3, D3's).** Every 15 s the manager reads `admin_dir` under the protected-file rule, as `scanAdmin` does (`adminrelay.go`), and when an `admin-request-*.json` file of kind `node.sensor_revoke` names a live sensor and is not yet decided, it **stops dialling that sensor and closes its connection** (`enrolment`), and raises `sensor-revoke-pending` while it holds. The payload is read for its `sensor_id` and pin only, before any signature is checked, so this can only stop a link, never start one or change state. It lifts if the collector refuses the request, if the file goes away undecided, or **when the request's own expiry passes** (D3), so a stale file cannot hold a link down while `collectorHold` stands; it becomes the revocation above if the collector accepts. No new file format. Test: `TestSensorRevokeFastPath` (a pending file stops the dial within 15 s; a refusal lifts it, and so does the request's expiry; control: the file ignored).
- **The local stop** (SL-9): "edit or remove the entry, and restart the node service". It needs no collector and no audit write, and leaves `service.start` in the chain. The restart pauses collection for the length of a start.

### Expiry and `pinned_by`

- `sensor-enrolment-expiring` from `CertWarning` (30 days, `certperiod.go`) before `not_after`, repeated hourly in status (HR-13's precedent). At `not_after`, `sensor-enrolment-expired`: a running link is not cut, and the next dial is refused, which writes `sensor.link_refused` with reason `expired` under the cap (§1.9 item 6).
- **`pinned_by`** (ruling 4): shown in status, with a standing alarm `sensor-pinned-by-console` while a live sensor reports `console`. No refusal.
- **Limit** (D3's condition 4): whoever can write the store directory can change a pin or remove a revocation with no signature. That is a compromised node (SL-5). Status shows each enrolment's `decision_seq` and payload hash, so that an off-host copy of the chain can be compared.

**Tests**, in the new package of §1.9, on the test clock: `TestSensorEnrolApplied`; `TestRenewCarriesPosition` (a `renew` with the pin unchanged: the old runs resume, not `run-regressed`; control: a node that reads the position as none at every enrolment, a `renew` included, which sends no `taken`, takes the run as new, and raises `sensor-gap-undeclared` for the records the agent had already trimmed); `TestReplaceCountsUnsentTail`; `TestReplaceTailAcrossRestart` (C13's H-11: a run whose last hello states `newest_seq` 120 with T at 100, the node restarted, then a `replace` whose old host never connects again: `sensor.run-loss` counts the old tail as 20; control: `newest_seq` kept in memory only, which counts 0 or writes nothing); `TestEditedEntryClosesShutdown` (CH-37's M-9 leg); `TestSensorEnrolDenied` for each of the seven codes in C8-2's order, and a request failing two checks carrying the earlier one's code; with `reason`: a `renew` with another pin, a `rekey` and a `replace` with the same pin, a `new` for a `sensor_id` that has an enrolment, and, with the state unknown, a `renew` and a `new`, each `denied` (`reason`) and nothing changed, while a `replace` with the state unknown is applied (control: the carry-over decided from the reason alone); with the link key's file unreadable, and with the state unread, each request stays pending and no `sensor.enrolled` is written (control: a node that denies `node-key` with no link key loaded uses up the request); **the three "never dropped" cases** (C8-3): a revocation while the enrolment is pending, after which the enrolment is `denied` (`revoked-key`); a revocation of generation 2's pin after a `rekey` to generation 3, after which the link stays up and an enrolment naming the old pin is `denied`; a revocation while the state is unread, after which the pin is in force from the acceptance on, in memory and before the state is read, and nothing dials it (control: the `no-enrolment` denial put back, under which each case ends with the revoked pin enrolled); and **a revocation folded while a collector reset is open, then the request's expiry passed: the node still dials nothing, and `sensor.revoked` is owed** (control: the fast path's hold, lifted at expiry, under which the node dials the revoked sensor); `TestSensorRevokeAppliedAtKept` (a `sensor.revoked` owed across a restart carries the `applied_at` of the mark); `TestSensorEnrolAuditFirst` (control: apply before the audit write, which then applies with the write failing); `TestSensorEnrolClosesOldConnection` (control: the old connection kept, which takes a record under the old pin), with a leg for a `renew` with the pin unchanged, which closes the open connection too (control: a node that closes only when the pin changes, which keeps it); `TestCarryOverStartGeneration` (SL-T10's carry-over leg: generation 1 a `new` with hellos accepted, generation 2 a `rekey` with none, generation 3 a `renew` of generation 2's pin; the first `node-hello` under generation 3 carries no `taken`, the run is accepted as new, no `sensor.link_refused` is written, and the pair's proof is saved with the run; controls: carry-over decided from the live generation's reason and pin alone, which closes `run-regressed` and writes the record; and a reset cursor written at the `rekey`, which with `sensorrun.json` unreadable reads the unanswered pair as proven and dials it past three); **and H-8's second leg in C13** (§1.3's owed rule above): a position that reads as none while it holds a span whose `lost` gap is still marked owed and a `prior` whose `sensor.run-loss` is still marked owed, the `rekey`'s first hello then accepted as a new run: both events are in the health stream, or both marks are in the new cursor, before the old cursor is replaced, and with the node killed between the writes and the save each is written once or twice, never not at all (control: a node that replaces the cursor before it writes or carries what it owes, which loses both); `TestSensorRevokeClosesFirst`, with the audit write made to fail; `TestSensorRevokeAfterReadLoop` (a record in commit at the revocation lands at or below `through_seq`; control: `through_seq` taken at the close); `TestSensorRevokeAlertThrough` (alerts raised later from earlier observations are at or below the alert `through_seq`; control: taken at the revocation); `TestSensorStateSurvivesPrune` (control: the consumer removed, which enrols a revoked pin again); `TestSensorStateOldFile` (a file below the cursor with a gap after it: unknown; control: folded anyway, which brings a revoked pin back); `TestSensorFoldErrorKeepsCollection` (control: the sensor fold joined to the suspension fold, which holds every run); `TestSensorUnparseableRecordDialsNothing` (a record in the fold that cannot be parsed while a sensor is live and connected: the open link closes with `state-unread`, no dial is made to any sensor afterwards, `sensor-state-unread` stands, and runs go on; control: a parseable record arriving the same way, after which the link stays open; and a node that keeps dialling the sensors it held live, which dials); `TestSensorStateReadErrorRetried`; `TestSensorCursorMovesAtFill` (an unparseable record with a small audit class: the cursor moves at three quarters and the revoked pins are written first; control: the cursor held, which fills the class); `TestSensorExpiry`. On the rig: SL-T4, SL-T5 with an expired, a revoked and a fork-held enrolment at start (the node starts, collects SER and Syslog, and alarms), SL-T10 with the node killed between the close and the audit write, and SL-T20's node leg (an enrolment that names the old node pin is `denied`, `node-key`).

## 1.4 Replay, resume and the acknowledgement: one position (from SL-13 to SL-16; D4's B1, D6's B3, ruling 6)

**One number.** The node's position for each sensor is the observation stream's source cursor, which C5 already has, and nothing else. The taken position T that every `ack` names, the `taken` that `node-hello` sends (CH-6) and the resume point after a restart are all that cursor's seq. **Requirement, not built:**

- **What it holds:** the generation, the run counter, the `run_id`, T, G and D of CH-15, and **the run's total refused and total dropped records** (ruling 6). **Under P4-4-L6 it also holds what changes with the run, so that nothing the node judges a sensor by is kept apart from D** (SL-31, ruling (b); D3's preference for `prior` and the digest; D5's choice on #641, comment 6836978459):
  - `write_no`, SL-30's write number (§1.1);
  - the run's `config_digest` (CH-32), saved at row 6;
  - **Ds,** the D at which the run's spans were last accepted (§1.6);
  - the last `newest_seq` the sensor stated for the run (§1.3, `replace`);
  - the run's heartbeat baselines: the last `frames_outgoing`, `drops_holder`, `frames_overlong` and `drops_driver` taken, and whether `egress_check` was carried (§1.8), or none (`drops_driver` by D1's rulings on #645, comments 6839936385 and 6840188059);
  - W, the moment the node holds as accounted for, with the `seq` and `at_utc` of the current heartbeat it came from, and the causes seen since (§1.9 item 5);
  - **with W** (SL-16 at #641's `da7a84ee`; D6's B1 and B2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`): R, the last current heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose with that interface's `carrier` not `down`, placed on the node's clock as W is (§1.9 item 5), and not moved while a gap is open, and never below the floor (§1.9 item 5); and, from W's heartbeat, each interface's `packets` and the `at_utc` of its last rise in the run (or of the run's first heartbeat, for an interface that never rose), all in the same writes as W. The interfaces are held as a count and then in the bytewise order of their names, the set the run's coverage statement names, which CH-31 holds fixed for the run (D5's form: names are not stored, since every heartbeat carries the coverage statement);
  - **the gap held for the floor** (D5's mechanism for SL-16's floor at #641's `cebfff61`, §1.9 item 5): at most one gap, other than `lost`, closed unwatched, whose raise of W and R is saved before its `sensor.coverage-gap` event has committed: its `from_utc` and `to_utc`, the cause at its open, its causes, the number of gaps it carries (a later gap closed unwatched while it waits is merged into it, §1.9 item 5), and one mark for `from_bound` and `clock_stepped`; each `-` when none is held;
  - the run's spans, at most 8, each as stated, with its cut end and its owed mark (§1.6, SL-31);
  - `prior`, when present: `runs_skipped`, `records_lost`, `previous_end`, `started_by`, its owed mark, and its spans, at most 8, each as above.
- **Its form and bound.** A fixed text form, `sp2` followed by those fields in that order, space-separated, with every decimal and every time (Unix nanoseconds) at most 20 digits, the digest at most 71 characters, and `-` for an absent field. At its largest it is **2,005 bytes**: 179 for the position (`sp2`, seven decimals and the `run_id`, 187 with its separators as before), 20 for `write_no`, 71 for the digest, 20 for Ds, 20 for `newest_seq`, 81 for the baselines (four counters of 20 digits and the one-character egress mark), 63 for W and its causes (a decimal of at most 3 digits), **341 for what is held with W** (20 for R, a one-digit count of interfaces, and for each of at most `MaxInterfaces` (8) interfaces its `packets` and its last rise's `at_utc`, 20 each), **65 for the gap held for the floor** (`from_utc` and `to_utc` of 20 each, the cause at its open 1, its causes a decimal of at most 3 digits, as W's are, the number of gaps it carries 20, and the one-character mark), 1 + 8 × 61 for the spans (a count, then for each span `from_utc`, `to_utc` and the cut end of 20 each and a one-character mark), 52 + 8 × 61 for `prior`, and 116 separators between its 117 fields. **The gap held for the floor adds 50 bytes** (45 and 5 separators) to the 1,934 of this note's head `1b815175`, the figure D3's list on #641 at `cebfff61` gives C13 CH-18 for #645; CH-18 needs the same change (D5's note for D3 and D4, on #641). **The number of gaps it carries adds 21 more** (20 and a separator; SL-16 at #641's `bb7f0d91`, D6's N4 on #641 at `02773aa1`), 71 in all; CH-18 needs the same change. **What is held with W adds 359 bytes** (341 and 18 separators) to the 1,575 of this note's head `e8ecd587`, which C13 CH-18 states at #645's `1c454cb6`; CH-18 needs the same change (D5's note for D4, on #641). The `drops_driver` baseline is the 21 bytes over the 1,554 of this note's head `4a606518` (one 20-digit decimal and its separator), which confirms C13 CH-18's 1,575 at #645's `d2362d7c`. **`prior`'s 52:** `runs_skipped` 20, `records_lost` 20, `previous_end` 1 (encoded `c` or `u`), `started_by` 9 (`enrolment`, the longest of C13's four), the owed mark 1, and the span count 1. Written out in C13's words, `previous_end` takes 7 (`unclean`) and the form 2,011 bytes (1,990 before the number of merged gaps, 1,940 before the gap held for the floor, 1,581 before what is held with W); 2,048 holds either way (`records-auditor` on #644). **`MaxLinkCursorBytes` = 2,048,** the figure C13 0.2 states with it (CH-18; it was 192 before P4-4-L6). The worst stored observation, about 34 KiB of the 64 KiB cap (C13 §10), keeps its room; R-5 runs with 2,048.
- **What it costs, stated** (`desk`: `contracts/store/commit.go` `Record.Bytes`; `platform/store/commit.go`). The store keeps a record's cursor in that record's row and counts it in the record's bytes and its class's quota. The link commits a record at a time, so every stored observation carries the cursor: at most 909 bytes with no span or `prior` held (888 before the number of merged gaps, 838 before the gap held for the floor, 479 before what is held with W, 187 before P4-4-L6), and up to 2,005 while they are held. The observation quota's sizing (Part 2 §3) counts it. A row of its own in the same transaction would cost no bytes per record, but it is a new table, which changes the store's format (`otsec.platform-store/2`, `platform/store/db.go`) and so C5; the brief excludes that.
- **When it is written:** in the same transaction as each committed observation (`Record.SourceCursor`, `contracts/store/commit.go`), and on its own with `store.DB.SaveSourceCursor` (`platform/store/outbox.go`, not part of C5) when nothing commits: **after a hold save,** a refusal, a drop, a duplicate or a gap, at most once a second (`AckMaxDelay`, CH-9; the list is CH-17's, D5's B5 on #634), **before every hold save** (below), **before a message that changes the spans, `prior`, the digest or a baseline is acted on** (row 5b, row 6, a hello or tick that raises D with a span, §1.6, §1.8), after each write of `sensorrun.json` (§1.1), and for W at most once a minute (§1.9 item 5). The value written is never below the position already saved, so a release that commits older held records never moves it back. Later record commits carry the same values unchanged, so the spans are never saved in a transaction apart from D's.
- **The `ack` never names more than the saved cursor.** A record is counted into T only once it is committed, saved in the hold's checkpoint, a duplicate, a gap, or refused or dropped for a final reason (D5-1) **with its total in a saved cursor.** So a node crash can never erase the only trace of a record the sensor has trimmed (ruling 2 on #627), and **no committed record is ever above the `taken` sent in `node-hello`**, so a resend is always a duplicate (CH-14).
- **A held record is never stored twice** (D6's item 3 on `4d03075a`, and D6 on `7f5b97d4`; D1's ruling to fix it, not state it). The case: a node killed between a hold save and the next cursor save restores its hold with record n while the saved T, and so `taken`, is below n. Two orders follow. The resend of n can arrive while the hold still holds n, or after the hold has released n: `Link.TickHold` releases a hold past its deadline whether or not a connection is open, and `Link.RecoverHold` commits at start a release the kill interrupted (`hold.go`). **The mechanism, which needs no new state (D1's option (a)):**
  1. **Before the hold saves record n, the node saves the position through n − 1** (`SaveSourceCursor`, with the run's totals). So every record below a held one is durably disposed of: committed, held, a duplicate, a gap, or refused or dropped with its total saved. The cost is one cursor save per held record. Held records are the flows between the node's own address and a run's endpoint, at most `HoldRecordCap` (23) a run (`hold.go`).
