The C4 event is built when it is written, with the binary's own `event.Identifier`, as every producer on `main` does. **Why:** a node's store refuses a pending event stamped with a later minor than its own (`assignSeq`, `platform/store/commit.go` l.353), so a held 0.4 event built before a rollback to a 0.3 binary would be refused, and lost. **On `main` no pending C4 event outlives the process that built it** (`desk`, `main` at `d59cda14`): every non-test use of `event.Identifier` (`normalize/ser.go` l.477 and l.519, `normalize/syslog.go` l.152, `platform/node/runtime.go` l.521, `platform/sensorlink/link.go` l.357) builds an event that is held only in memory until its commit, and none of the files the node writes outside its databases (`status`, `suspensions`, `nodekeys`, `findings`, a quarantine export's manifest) holds a pending event; the intake's hold checkpoint (`netcheckpoint.intake`) keeps `HeldRecord.Observation`, the observation's body, and `Link.record` builds the event at commit; the node's `deferred` records (`runtime.go`, at most `MaxDeferredAudit`) are audit-class records in memory, which the store seals as C8 and never passes to `assignSeq` (`commit.go` l.84 to l.117), as it does configuration-class documents. Test: E4-7's node leg, `TestHeldPoolEventBuiltAtWrite` (a `sensor.counters` and a `sensor.redial-granted` held past the ceiling in `sensorrun.json`, the file read back: no C4 event bytes in it; with room again, the events written carry the writing binary's `event.Identifier`; control: a node that stores the built event in the file, whose held event, re-stamped by a test seam to a later minor, the store then refuses).

**Coverage gaps** (D4-90's gap rule; SL-16 and C8-7 as #641 amends them; CH-45; D4's B4 on #633). **Every interval the platform did not watch is a gap, on the node's clock, whatever caused it, and the rule fails closed:** what the node cannot show watched is recorded as unwatched.

- **The causes, one closed set of nine,** the same tokens in CH-45, SL-16 and C8-7:

  | Cause | The node opens it |
  | --- | --- |
  | `node-start` | At its start, for each configured sensor, from the earlier of W and R (below). It takes the node's own downtime and whatever the sensor did meanwhile, so a station power loss that stopped both machines leaves no interval in no gap |
  | `link` | When a connection whose hello was accepted ends, or a dial fails, while no gap is open: from the earlier of W and R. It takes the sensor host down, a network fault and a sensor never back. **And while the node holds no W for a sensor, from the live enrolment's `enrolled_at`** (SL-16 at #641's `9d74bb73`; D3's ruling (b), comment 6839793790 on #644): a sensor never watched holds this gap from its enrolment, whether or not a connection has ended, and it closes at the first current heartbeat that shows capture, always with `covered: false` (below) |
  | `restart` | Never at the open: a `link`, `node-start` or `silent` gap becomes `restart` when the node reads, before the gap closes, the hello of a run that began inside it. It takes a sensor restart during an outage. **The sensor host down** shows as `link` while it lasts and as `restart` once the next run's hello is read; the node cannot tell it from a network fault by itself |
  | `silent` (D5's token, comment 6840288076 on #645, which CH-45 takes) | **A heartbeat silence on a link that stays up** (SL-16's ninth cause at #641's `467c1dd6`; D6's B1 on #641 at `9d74bb73`): while no gap is open and the link is current, no current heartbeat for `HeartbeatGapMax` (30 s) on the node's monotonic clock, counted from the receipt of W's heartbeat: from the earlier of W and R. Ticks are unbuffered and keep the link inside `IdleTimeout`, so a wedged agent or buffer stops the heartbeats with no connection's end, and without this the next current heartbeat would move W across the silence. Written `open` once it has stood 60 s from W, as a `link` gap is; closed as covered only by the chain (below), and a stall leaves it unwatched; its cause ends at the first current heartbeat whose lateness (CH-23) is under `HeartbeatGapMax` (D6's N1 on #641 at `467c1dd6`); it becomes `restart` as a `link` gap does; and when it and a connection's end fall due at the same evaluation, one gap opens, with cause `link`, while if it opens first the end adds `link` to `causes`. `sensor-no-heartbeat` (CH-44) is unchanged: the alarm says the heartbeats stopped, and the gap carries the interval |
  | `held` | From the earlier of W and R, when the node stops dialling a sensor, or closes its link, on a decision of its own other than a revocation: a fork hold, the dial limit, the state or position unread, the store not open, the intake not started, the link key, a pending revocation file, an enrolment the configuration does not match or that has expired, an asset's address, the platform floor, and a `renew`'s close until the sensor's end holds it |
  | `revoked` | From the earlier of W and R, at a revocation's close (§1.3). **Closed only by the first current heartbeat, showing capture, of a run under a later enrolment's generation** (a new key, since a revoked pin is never enrolled again), **and always as unwatched** (`covered: false`): no chain runs across a new generation's run 1, and nothing the revoked key sent is trusted for the interval. Not by a restart, a change of configuration or time; with no later enrolment it stays open (SL-16 at `41a073f2`; D3's rule 1, posted first as comment 6838033945 on #641) |
  | `sensor-failed` | From the earlier of W and R, when a recovered panic stops the sensor's work until a restart (§1.9 item 9; D3's addition in #641) |
  | `lost` | For each new or widened span (CH-40, SL-31; §1.6). Written once, `closed` with `covered: false` and its span's times (below) |
  | `blind` | When every interface is blind (CH-31), from the earlier of W and R (below; R is the last current heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose with that interface's `carrier` not `down`) (§1.8; SL-16 at #641's `da7a84ee`; D6's B2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`). Written `open` from that start, not from the moment the node finds every interface blind, and closed `covered: false` at the first current heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rise |

- **Where a gap starts** (SL-16 at #641's `8f78d057`; D6's B3 on #641 at `9d74bb73`, B2 at `467c1dd6`, B1 and N2 at `da7a84ee`). **Every gap the node opens, while it runs or at its start, starts at the earlier of W and R,** whatever its cause and whenever the node notices the cause. A sensor host that loses power sends no FIN, so the node sees the loss only at `IdleTimeout` (30 s, CH-4); a gap opened at the close would leave that 30 s, and up to a heartbeat interval before it, in no gap. W can run up to `blind_after_s` ahead of R, so a flat stretch that began before W began at R. R does not move while a gap is open, so the start is fixed at the open, and a gap that never closes is written `open` from it. **The floor** (SL-16 at #641's `cebfff61`; D6's B1 on #641 at `8f78d057`). When a gap other than `lost` closes unwatched (`covered: false`), W and R become its `to_utc` wherever they are earlier or have no value, and the floor becomes that `to_utc` whatever it was before, and after that neither takes a value below it (a new W or R below it is taken at it), the move back of a falling E included (below). It applies only at an unwatched close. So no gap other than `lost`, and other than one that starts at a bound, starts before the `to_utc` of an earlier gap closed unwatched, where no step back of the node's wall clock lies between the two gaps; without the floor, a later gap could start at an earlier gap's start and, closed as covered, claim with the same `from_utc` an interval already written unwatched. On a run flat from its first heartbeat with no R, the gap before it closes unwatched at that heartbeat, so the floor gives R a value and the `blind` gap starts no later than that gap's end. The floor is held in memory, and W and R are saved at or above it, so it adds no field; at a node start it is where the `node-start` gap begins, and the step check at that gap's close caps it (below). A step back of the wall clock moves it back with W, R and the copy (below). **W, R and the floor are never later than the node's wall clock** (D6's B1 on #641 at `cebfff61`). **The step rule** (SL-16 at #641's `bb7f0d91`; D1's direction on D6's B1 on #641 at `02773aa1`). Before the node takes a time from its wall clock for a gap (its open, its close, its `from_utc` or `to_utc`, the floor's raise, a `lost` gap's cut end, the time a never-watched gap starts from), for W, R or the floor (a heartbeat's receipt, and the ticks' receipts that E is taken from), or for the run file's copy, it runs the step check, and it runs it at its tick as well. The check compares the wall clock with the monotonic clock since the check before. **It acts only on a step back of more than `max_skew_ms` (2,000 ms) between two checks;** a smaller difference moves nothing and marks nothing, so a time service that slews the clock does not mark every gap open through the slew (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D6's N3 on #641 at `6723bf90`). A step back found moves W, R, the floor, the copy and E's held samples back by the step. The node saves the cursor and the run file at once, writes `time.node-stepped` once for that step, and marks `clock_stepped` on any gap open when the step is found, the gap being opened or closed included, and on any gap closed and not yet written: the gap held for the floor, and a gap waiting for its budget or the ceiling (D6's N1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). Then any of W, R and the floor still later than the wall clock is a step back the check could not measure: it takes the wall-clock time, and is marked and written in the same way. Only then is the time taken. A gap's written `from_utc` is never moved. **A gap marked `clock_stepped` is never closed as covered by the chain:** it closes unwatched (`covered: false`), at the first current heartbeat after its cause ends that shows capture (D1's ruling on #641 at `bb7f0d91`). **At a start** the check cannot measure a step made during the stop, so it can only cap, **and the cap waits for the `node-start` gap's close.** While that gap is open, nothing places W or R and no other gap opens. A cap saved then would move W past the stop that the gap holds, and a kill before the gap is written would lose the stop. The check at its close caps W, R and the floor before its `to_utc` is taken, and a check at a tick before then only measures. **The comparison at the start marks the `node-start` gap `clock_stepped` at once** where W, R or the floor is later than the wall clock; the mark moves nothing, so the gap's `open` record names the step, and only the cap waits (D6's B1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). W and R are capped as the floor is, because a gap starts at the earlier of them and the floor's raise lifts each only where it is earlier. Capping R alone would be enough, since R has a value whenever W has one; the check treats every value it moves alike. A crash between the close and the save of the raise must leave neither the gap lost nor W and R below its end: the closing event is in another database, so the raise is saved first, in a cursor save that holds the closed gap in a field of its own, the gap held for the floor (§1.4), and the event commits after it; the first cursor save after that commit clears the field. **At a start, a gap found in that field is written first,** `closed` with `covered: false` and its own times and marks, before any other gap, and the field is cleared once it commits. A kill then writes the gap twice at worst, and never not at all, and W and R are never saved below its end. **The run file's copy is the earliest of W, R and the `from_utc` of the gap held for the floor, as the node holds them when it writes the file, and the gap is held from the raise itself, before the raise is saved** (SL-16 at #641's `bb7f0d91`; D6's N1 on #641 at `02773aa1`). A lost sensor database loses the field with the cursor, so a copy of the raised W and R alone, written after the raise and before the commit, would leave the held gap in no gap. **While a gap is held there, the copy never rises:** each write of the run file takes the earlier of the new value and the copy it replaces, since a step back moves W, R and the copy but not the held gap's `from_utc` (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D6's N2 on #641 at `6723bf90`). An event refused past its budget or the ceiling stays in the field until it is written. **One gap is held there at a time, from the raise until its event commits:** a gap other than `lost` that closes unwatched while the held gap's event has not committed is merged into it, as gaps past a budget are (below). Its causes are added, the held gap's `to_utc` becomes its `to_utc`, its number of gaps rises by one, the merged gap carries `clock_stepped` if either part carries it and `from_bound` as the held gap does (D6's N1 on #641 at `6723bf90`), and the raise to that end is saved with the merged field. While the held gap's event waits, the node writes no record of a later gap of the same sensor other than `lost` ("Budgets and merging", below), so a gap merged into it was never written `open` (D6's B3 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`). One event is written for the whole, with `merged` that number. Once the held gap's event has committed, the next gap closed unwatched takes the field (SL-16 at #641's `bb7f0d91`; D6's N4 on #641 at `02773aa1`: held in W, the later gap let a gap opened meanwhile start inside it). The watched interval between the two is written inside the merged gap, which fails closed. **Why a field** (D5's choice, which D3 leaves to D5 on #641 at `cebfff61`): a gap waiting for its budget is held in W, which does not move past it (above), and that cannot hold a gap whose raise has already moved W past its start; so the gap held across the raise needs its own times and its number of merged gaps, 71 bytes of the cursor (§1.4). **Two exceptions:** a `lost` gap is its span (below), which can begin after W, and is written with its span's times; and a gap opened while the node holds no W starts at the bound (below: the run file's copy of the earlier of W and R, or `enrolled_at`), with `from_bound: true`. **What it costs:** a gap after a flat stretch shorter than `blind_after_s` writes up to that stretch of watched time inside the gap, which fails closed. A gap closed as covered is unchanged in substance, since the chain's part 2 fails wherever a flat stretch reaches `blind_after_s` and the floor keeps every gap closed unwatched out of [R, W], and its part 4 counts the length from W, never from R (below). The 60 s after which a `link`, `node-start` or `silent` gap is written `open` are counted from W. *Test:* `TestGapNeverBackOpen`'s second case (leg 10): no heartbeat after the last current one, ticks continuing, and the connection reset 20 s after that heartbeat's receipt: `from_utc` is no later than that receipt, cause `link`. *Control:* a node with the ninth cause intact that opens `link` at the close starts it 20 s late. `TestGapHeldOpen`, `TestGapRevokedOpen` and `TestGapSensorFailedOpen` (legs 11 to 13) each gain the same case, with the fork's second connection, the applied revocation or the panic 20 s after that receipt. *Control:* a gap opened at its decision starts 20 s late. `TestGapHeartbeatSilence`'s fourth case (leg 16) and `TestGapFlatCountsCarrierUp`'s fifth to seventh cases (leg 17) test R; `TestGapNeverBackOpen`'s third case (leg 10) and `TestGapFlatCountsCarrierUp`'s eighth case (leg 17) test the start at the open across a gap that never closes and across a new run; `TestGapHeartbeatSilence`'s fifth case (leg 16) and `TestGapFlatCountsCarrierUp`'s ninth case (leg 17) test the bound on W and R; `TestGapHeartbeatSilence`'s sixth to eighth cases (leg 16), `TestGapNeverWatchedFromEnrolment`'s second case (leg 15), `TestGapHeldInW`'s second to fourth cases (leg 14) and `TestGapLostDatabaseReachesBack`'s fifth to tenth cases (leg 9) test the floor. So a sensor host that loses power, which sends no FIN and is seen only at `IdleTimeout` (30 s, CH-4), leaves neither that 30 s nor the heartbeat interval before it in no gap. **Within a run, a gap's length is taken on the node's monotonic clock,** and its written times on the wall clock (D6's N1 on #641): a step forward while a gap is open does not change the count the chain's fourth part asks for, and a step back is found by the step check and marks the gap, which then closes unwatched (D1's ruling on #641 at `bb7f0d91`). A step back that puts the written end before the start is handled as across a restart (below): **a gap is never dropped for its times,** and it names the step.

- **W, the moment accounted through,** kept in the cursor (§1.4): the node's own time through which every interval was either watched or written in a committed gap event. While no gap is open, W moves, at each **current heartbeat**, to the earlier of the node's receipt of it and its `at_utc` + E (CH-22's E as it stands at the receipt, its samples moved back by any step back found since each was taken, under the step rule above; SL-16 at #641's `8f78d057`; D6's B2 on #641 at `da7a84ee`), never below the floor (a value below it is taken at it) and never later than the node's wall clock (above). The heartbeat is one read on a connection whose hello was accepted, of the run that is current (CH-7) once the backlog is through (CH-39), not late (CH-23), and **showing capture: not every interface blind under CH-31 at its receipt, flat counts included** (some interface's `carrier` is not `down` and its `packets` rose within the last `blind_after_s` of the node's monotonic time, or its run began within it, since CH-31 starts its comparison again at a run's first heartbeat; SL-16 at #641's `467c1dd6`, D6's B2 on #641). The carrier alone is not enough: under it, a cut with every `packets` flat and the carrier `up` would close as covered while CH-44 records the same stretch as `sensor.past-blind`. That receipt is a moment the node saw itself (D3's condition 1); a time the sensor states is not one, **the heartbeat's own `at_utc` included, however close it is to the receipt** (D6's B3 on #641: a sensor clock five minutes ahead makes no heartbeat late), nor a backlog record's commit time. `at_utc` + E serves only to take W, or R, below the receipt, never above it. **Why the bound** (SL-16; D6's B2 on #641 at `da7a84ee`). A heartbeat stays current up to `late_bound_s` late (CH-23). Heartbeats queue in the agent's buffer and ticks do not, so a slow intake can deliver every heartbeat about two minutes late without the ninth cause falling due. Placed at their receipts, W and R would lie that much after the moment their heartbeat shows, and that interval would be in no gap. **The residual** is the delay E itself takes in: the least, over the last six ticks, of the network delay and the queue in the stream (CH-22). **A sensor that lies gains nothing new.** It sets `at_utc` + E, but the bound only takes W and R below the receipt. So it can move them only earlier, which widens a gap, or decline the bound by stating a late `at_utc`, which gives back that delay and no more than stating heartbeats that show capture already gives it (AP-36). W and R take each new value even when it is earlier than the one before, since E can fall, and a gap starts at the earlier of the two either way. The one limit is the floor (above): no move back takes W or R below the end of a gap closed unwatched, since a move below it would start a later gap inside that gap, and a sensor that states early `at_utc`s could do it at will. *Test:* `TestGapHeartbeatSilence`'s fifth case and `TestGapFlatCountsCarrierUp`'s ninth case. The node keeps the heartbeat's `seq` and `at_utc` with W. **With no W, a gap opened while the node runs begins at the same bound as one opened at a start** (below). **W does not move past an open gap** until that gap's closing event has committed or the gap is closed as covered, except at an unwatched close, whose raise is saved with the gap held for the floor before its event commits (above). W is saved with every cursor save, and on its own at most once a minute when nothing commits, so the gap after a start may begin up to 60 s before the last heartbeat read: it reaches back **no later than** that heartbeat (D5's N4 on #641), which errs towards a longer gap. With W the cursor keeps, from W's heartbeat, each interface's `packets` and the `at_utc` of its last rise in the run (or of the run's first heartbeat, for an interface that never rose), and R, all in the same writes as W; while a gap is open, R does not move, as W does not (§1.4; SL-16 at #641's `da7a84ee`). **A copy of the earlier of W and R is also kept in `sensorrun.json`,** written with each write of that file and never on its own, as the earliest of W, R and the `from_utc` of the gap held for the floor as the node holds them, the gap held from the raise itself (above); it serves only when the sensor database is lost (below), where an older copy only moves a gap's start earlier. With the cursor lost, a copy of W alone would leave the stretch before W in no gap. **On a step back of the wall clock seen while the node runs** (the step rule, above), the node moves W, R, the floor, the copy and E's held samples back by the step, saves them at once, marks the gap it affects `clock_stepped`, and writes `time.node-stepped` once (D6's N2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`). *Test:* leg 9's fourth, fifth and eighth to tenth cases, in `TestGapLostDatabaseReachesBack`, and leg 16's eighth case, in `TestGapHeartbeatSilence` (below).
- **Closed as covered** (D3's condition 2 and SL-16 as #641 states it at `41a073f2`; D5's N5 on #641). A gap of any cause but `lost`, `blind` and `revoked` (SL-16 at `41a073f2` names the three that no chain closes: a `lost` gap is its declared span, a `blind` gap closes unwatched when an interface's `packets` rise again, and a `revoked` gap closes unwatched at a later generation's first current heartbeat, as in the table above), and not marked `clock_stepped` (D1's ruling on #641 at `bb7f0d91`: a gap so marked closes unwatched), is closed as covered, whole, only by **a chain of heartbeats in one run, from W's heartbeat to a current heartbeat after the gap,** whose two ends the node placed itself, never later than its own receipt, and only if all four hold:
  1. every `seq` of the run between the two ends is read, with no rise of D among them;
  2. **the chain shows capture:** each heartbeat of the chain shows capture under CH-31 applied by `at_utc`: some interface's `carrier` is not `down` in that heartbeat, and that interface's `packets` rose within the `blind_after_s` before it, by `at_utc`, where a rise is a `packets` above the same interface's in the run's heartbeat before. It is never read as CH-31's windowed state over a stretch. The chain looks back to what the cursor keeps with W (above): each interface's `packets` at W's heartbeat and the `at_utc` of its last rise in the run, or of the run's first heartbeat for an interface that never rose; where the node holds neither for an interface, or the values held do not match the interfaces of the run's coverage statement one for one, the chain fails, and it never counts from the chain's own first heartbeat. A chain that fails leaves the whole gap open, unwatched, from its start, which is already the earlier of W and R (above), so it agrees with `sensor.past-blind` (CH-44) before W as well as after (SL-16 at #641's `da7a84ee`; D6's B1 and B2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`);
  3. **no two consecutive heartbeats are more than `HeartbeatGapMax` (30 s) apart by their `at_utc`.** The two ends count, so a silence just before the reconnect fails too; only `at_utc` enters this check, and where the node places a live heartbeat on its own clock (W, above) does not;
  4. **there are at least as many heartbeats as the gap's length on the node's clock divided by `HeartbeatGapMax`:** 20 for a ten-minute gap. The length is counted from W, never from the gap's start at R, since the chain begins at W's heartbeat. This ties the sensor's spacing to the node's clock, so a sensor clock running slow cannot pass the third part with too few heartbeats (D3 on #644, comment 6837478350).

  The spacing and the count rest on the sensor's word, as every heartbeat does (D3; AP-36); they stop an honest fault, such as an engine that went quiet without a new run, from closing a gap by its `seq`s alone. **`HeartbeatGapMax` is three of C13's 10 s `SensorTickInterval`s,** the figure that C13 §12 defines for the 0.1 heartbeat; the figure depends on the 0.2 heartbeat's cadence, which D4 states in CH-39 with `HeartbeatGapMax` as the one value of the three documents. **Otherwise** the gap closes at the first current heartbeat after its cause ends **that shows capture** (as W's heartbeat must, above), and is written as unwatched from its start to that heartbeat's receipt; a sensor whose counts are still flat at the reconnect keeps the gap open until an interface's `packets` rise. **There is no partial cover:** whatever the chain does not show, the whole gap stays unwatched. For a break longer than `backlog_tie_s` that is D3's condition, adopted by D1 as a refinement of D4-90 (comment 6837169700 on #641): "covered" must be shown, and across a sensor restart inside such a break it cannot be. **This note applies it to shorter breaks too, which is wider than the ruling;** it fails closed (watched time at worst reported as unwatched), and D3 accepts it in SL-16. Where the chain shows coverage, D4-90's sentence holds unchanged. So a quiet interval that was watched closes; a restart, a declared loss, an unread `seq`, a silent stretch or too few heartbeats leaves the whole gap open. A `blind` gap closes at the first current heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose; a `lost` gap is its span (CH-40's times: the stated `from_utc` and the cut end).
- **What is written:** the health event `sensor.coverage-gap` on `platform/health`, with the details `cause` (at the open), `causes` (every cause seen while it stood, space-separated), `state` (`open` or `closed`), `from_utc`, and when closed `to_utc` and `covered` (`true` or `false`), the times on the node's clock except a `lost` gap's; `merged`, the number of gaps a merged record carries; `from_bound: true` when `from_utc` is an earlier bound and not W (below); and `clock_stepped: true` when the node's clock was stepped back across the gap (below). **`from_bound` and `clock_stepped` are D5's names** (SL-16 at `41a073f2` leaves them, and `enrolled_at`, to D5 and states only what each must carry), which CH-45 states. **When:** **a `lost` gap is written once, `closed` with `covered: false` and its span's times,** from the save that admits its span (SL-16 at #641's `9d74bb73`: "written once with those times"; CH-45). Both its ends are known when the span is admitted, since the cut end is fixed then (§1.6), and no heartbeat makes it covered, so an `open` record would say nothing the closed one does not. This text first wrote it `open` and then `closed`, two records (D4's item (a) on #645). A `blind`, `held`, `revoked` or `sensor-failed` gap is written `open` when it opens; a `link` or `node-start` gap is written `open` only once it has stood 60 s from W, the never-watched gap 60 s from `enrolled_at`, as `sensor-unreachable` is raised, and a `silent` gap once it has stood 60 s from W; **and each no later than 60 s after it opened on the node's monotonic clock,** since across a restart, or from `enrolled_at`, no step moves the wall-clock time it counts from (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D6's B1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). Every gap written `open` is written again when it closes, covered or not; one closed unwatched before it was written `open` is written once, `closed`. So a link drop whose backlog covers it within a minute writes nothing, and a longer one is forwarded as open and then as covered: no gap that closes as covered is forwarded as unwatched (C8-7's leg 2). Watched time can still lie inside a gap closed unwatched: the stretch from R to W that its start takes in, and whatever a failed chain leaves whole, since there is no partial cover. Both fail closed.
- **Budgets and merging.** `lost` gaps have their own 4 an hour, and every other cause another 4 (above). Past a budget, gaps of that budget are merged into one record: first start, last end, number and causes, and `clock_stepped` if any of them carries it (D6's N1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). **A `lost` gap merges only with `lost` gaps,** so it is never merged away without its cause. A gap waiting for its budget or the ceiling is held in the cursor, as above. **The order of a sensor's gap records, and how a reader pairs them** (SL-16 at #641's `e164e416`; D6's B3 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`, which replaces the pairing by interval; D6's B2 and N4 on #641 at `50a67730`). While the closing record of a gap other than `lost` waits, held for the floor or waiting for its budget or the ceiling, the node writes no record of a later gap of the same sensor other than `lost`, so a gap merged into a waiting record was never written `open`; a waiting `lost` record holds nothing back, and one sensor's waiting record holds back no other sensor's. A `closed` record pairs with every `open` record of the same sensor, written before it and not yet paired, whose `from_utc` equals its own; nothing pairs by interval. While the node runs there is at most one such record; across a node stop there can be two, a gap written `open` and still open at the stop and the `node-start` gap from the same earlier of W and R, and the `node-start` gap's close ends both. An `open` record that no record pairs reads as a gap still open, so nothing reads as closed that its own record did not close. The 60 s by which a gap is written `open` yields to this order as it yields to the ceiling and the budget.
- **At a start,** the node opens `node-start` from the earlier of W and R for each configured sensor, and writes the `lost` gap of every span still marked owed (§1.6).
  - **With no W in the cursor, W is unknown, and the gap reaches back** whatever the reason: the cursor unread, or the sensor database lost (under a live enrolment, a missing database is a lost one, §1.1). **A sensor never watched since its enrolment has been unwatched since it, so the same reach-back holds for every gap opened for it, at a node start or while the node runs** (SL-16 at `41a073f2`): the reach-back applies whenever the node holds no W for the sensor, not for a lost database only. **`enrolled_at` is D5's name** for the record time SL-16 cites. It reaches back to **an earlier moment the node still holds durably, never to a later one** (SL-16 at `41a073f2`; gap leg 9; D3 on #644, comment 6837478350): the copy of the earlier of W and R in `sensorrun.json` when that file holds one, whatever its `write_no`, since a file that is behind only moves the start earlier; and otherwise the time of the live enrolment's `sensor.enrolled` record, which `sensors.json` keeps beside its `decision_seq` as `enrolled_at` and the fold can read from the chain. The event carries `from_bound: true`, which says that `from_utc` is a bound and not the moment watching stopped, and status says so. So a station power loss that also takes the sensor database leaves no interval in no gap. *Both*, because D3 leaves the choice to D5 and the copy is free: it is written only with writes the file makes anyway.
  - **A sensor never watched holds a gap from its enrolment, not from a connection's end** (SL-16 at #641's `9d74bb73`; D3's ruling (b), comment 6839793790 on #644, on D5's question 6839551239). A `link` gap otherwise opens only when a connection ends or a dial fails, so a sensor whose first connection is accepted at once would leave the interval from its enrolment to its first current heartbeat in no gap, and through a long first backlog, or a first dial that never comes, nothing would be `open` in the stream: a quiet stream would read as a watched sensor, which fails open under D4-90. So **while the node holds no W for a sensor, it holds a gap for it from the live enrolment's `enrolled_at`,** with cause `link`, or `node-start` when the node finds it so at a start. It is opened when the enrolment is applied (§1.3, step 6) or at the start, and written as any `link` gap is: `open` once it has stood 60 s and again when it closes, or once, `closed`, if it closes sooner. **It closes at the first current heartbeat that shows capture, always with `covered: false`:** no W exists for a chain to start from, and a backlog the sensor made before that heartbeat is its word, placed on no clock of the node's. That heartbeat then sets W. **Its event carries `from_bound: true`,** as every reach-back here does, so one rule covers a lost database and a sensor never watched; `from_utc` is then `enrolled_at` itself, the node's own record time and never a later moment, so the bound is exact in this case and never says that watching stopped later than it did (D3 leaves the detail to D5).
  - **Across a node restart the reach-back compares wall-clock times,** the saved moment and the time after the start, since nothing else survives a restart (as for C8-5's cap). A clock stepped forward during the outage lengthens the gap, which fails closed. **A clock stepped back** can put the gap's end before its start: the gap is still written from the saved moment, never dropped, with `clock_stepped: true`, and the node writes C4's `time.node-stepped` health event once for that step (SL-16; D5 on #641). The step check at the `node-start` gap's close then caps W, R and the floor at the wall clock (above). **Across a restart the chain's fourth part counts the length from the saved W, a wall-clock difference.** A step back that the cap finds marks the `node-start` gap, which then closes unwatched (D1's ruling on #641 at `bb7f0d91`). **What is left** is a step back that leaves the saved W no later than the clock after the start. It can be as long as the stop plus W's age at the stop, up to a minute since the cursor is saved at most a minute apart, and asks the chain for at most that time divided by 30 s fewer heartbeats; parts 1 to 3 still hold. Where one source serves both hosts, a step back it makes on both during the stop raises no `sensor-clock-skew`, and a stall of the sensor no longer than that step can then close as covered, within the same bound (SL-16 at #641's `50a67730`; D6's N3 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`). Only wall-clock times survive a reboot; across a restart of the process alone the host's boot-relative clock survives, and not using it is D3's choice, on cost (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D6's N4 on #641 at `6723bf90`). C4 0.3 defines `time.node-stepped` (`contracts/event/event.go`); nothing on `main` writes it, so this is a new writer and not a C4 change.
- **Tests,** C8-7's seventeen gap legs as #641 states them at `e164e416` (leg 14 is this note's `TestGapHeldInW`), on the test clock in `platform/node/sensortest`, each with its control. **How a leg cuts the link** (D6's N6 on #641 at `467c1dd6`): unless a leg says otherwise, the connection is reset at the cut, with no heartbeat withheld before it, and every dial is refused until the cut ends.
  - `TestGapNodeAndSensorStopped` (leg 1; control: a gap that begins at the node's start, which leaves the outage in none; **second case**, D6's B3 on #641: the test source's clock five minutes ahead of the node's: `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt of the last current heartbeat; control: a node that keeps the heartbeat's `at_utc` as W, which starts the gap five minutes late);
  - `TestGapLinkCutCovered` (leg 2: a 10-minute cut while the sensor captures, one run with a heartbeat every interval: closed as covered, no `closed` record with `covered: false`; control: closed at the reconnect, before the backlog is through);
  - `TestGapQuietLinkCovered` (leg 3 at `467c1dd6`: no observations, while every interface's `packets` keeps rising, frames the engine names nothing for: closed as covered on the heartbeats; control: closing only on observations, which leaves it open. "No traffic on the monitored link", its first wording, is a blind stretch under CH-31 and must not close as covered; leg 17 tests that);
  - `TestGapHostStoppedStaysOpen` (leg 4: the sensor host stopped for five of the ten minutes: the whole ten minutes stay open, cause `restart`; control: the whole break closed at the reconnect);
  - `TestGapRestartInLongBreak` (leg 5; control: closed on the heartbeats' stated times);
  - `TestGapLostOwnBudget` (leg 6, the test source starting a new run at each kill, with one declared loss among the drops written with cause `lost`; control: one budget for every cause, which merges it away; **second case, a merge under a budget** (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D5's third point and D6's N1 on #641 at `6723bf90`): the budget of the causes other than `lost` spent within the hour but for one record; a `link` gap that stands two minutes is written `open` with that record, the node's wall clock is stepped back five minutes while it is open, and it closes unwatched past the budget; two more gaps follow, each closing unwatched within 30 s with a sensor restart inside it, and are merged with it: once the budget allows, one record holds the three, from the first gap's start, `merged` 3, `covered: false`, `clock_stepped: true`, and it pairs with the first gap's `open` record, so no `open` record of the leg is left unpaired; controls: a node that writes a merge under a budget from the last gap's start, which leaves the first gap's `open` record unpaired, and a node that keeps the last gap's mark only, which writes no `clock_stepped`; **third case, a waiting `lost` record and another sensor's waiting record** (D6's B2 on #641 at `50a67730`): the `lost` budget spent within the hour, so a fifth `lost` record waits for it; then a `link` gap of the same sensor stands 90 s: its `open` record is in the health stream within 60 s plus one node tick of its open; the same with a second sensor, whose budget of the causes other than `lost` is spent and whose closing record waits, and a `link` gap of the first sensor that stands 90 s: its `open` record is in the stream within the same bound; controls: a node that holds every later gap behind any waiting record of the same sensor, which writes the first `open` record only when the `lost` budget returns, and a node that holds a sensor's later gaps behind another sensor's waiting record, which writes the second only when that sensor's budget returns);
  - `TestGapSilentEngineStaysOpen` (leg 7: one run with unbroken `seq`s and no heartbeat for eight minutes of a ten-minute break: open; and a 40 s silence just before the reconnect, between the last buffered heartbeat and the first live one: open; controls: the chain judged by `seq`s alone, which closes the first, and a spacing check that leaves out the chain's live end, which closes the second);
  - `TestGapChainCount` (leg 8: a ten-minute gap, the sensor's clock at about half speed, 15 heartbeats 20 s apart by `at_utc`: open, since 20 are needed; control: a chain checked by spacing alone, which closes it);
  - `TestGapLostDatabaseReachesBack` (leg 9: the sensor database deleted while the node and the sensor are stopped for ten minutes: the start gap reaches back to no later than the run file's copy (the earlier of W and R), and with that file also deleted to the live enrolment's `enrolled_at`, with `from_bound: true`. In that second case the database is still lost, since the enrolment is live (§1.1): the node holds no run copy, so it sends no `taken`, and SL-30's counts read as spent: no source port until a re-arm, and `sensor-dial-limit` stands, as SL-T23 (p)'s third case checks; and a clock stepped back across the outage: the gap is written from the saved moment with `clock_stepped: true`, and `time.node-stepped` is written; controls: a gap that begins at the node's start, which leaves the ten minutes in none; and a gap dropped when its end is before its start; **third case, with the node running** (D6's N1 on #641; D1's ruling on #641 at `bb7f0d91`): a ten-minute link cut in one run with a full chain, the wall clock stepped back fifteen minutes five minutes into it: the gap closes with `covered: false` and `clock_stepped: true`, and `time.node-stepped` is written once; control: a node that lets the chain close a stepped gap as covered, which writes `covered: true`; and the same cut with a full chain and the wall clock stepped forward thirty minutes five minutes into it: the gap closes as covered; control: a node that takes the gap's length from its wall-clock times, which asks the chain for 80 heartbeats, holds about 60, and leaves the gap open; **fourth case** (D6's N2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`): the wall clock stepped back five minutes while the node runs, then a joint stop two minutes later with the sensor database deleted; the test makes the node write `sensorrun.json` while it holds W, one minute before the step (an alarm raised, say), and nothing else writes that file between that write and the stop: `from_utc` is no later than the last current heartbeat's receipt on the new clock; control: W and its copy kept on the old clock, which start the gap at that copy, about two minutes late (less up to one heartbeat interval); **fifth case, the floor and a step back** (D6's B1 on #641 at `8f78d057`): a `silent` gap closes unwatched one minute before the node's wall clock is stepped back five minutes, heartbeats current after it, and two minutes after the step the node and the sensor stop together and start again, the sensor database kept: the `node-start` gap's `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt, on the new clock, of the last current heartbeat; control: a node that moves W, R and the copy back with the step but not the floor, which holds W and R at the `silent` gap's end on the old clock and starts the gap about two minutes after that receipt; **sixth case, the floor after a step during a stop** (D6's B1 on #641 at `cebfff61`; D6's N2 at `02773aa1`; D1's ruling at `bb7f0d91`): heartbeats current every 10 s with `packets` rising; the node alone stopped for one minute, the sensor capturing and its database kept, and the node's clock stepped back five minutes during the stop; the `node-start` gap, marked `clock_stepped` by the cap at its close, closes with `covered: false` once the backlog is through, and the connection is reset 30 s after that close: the `node-start` gap's record carries `covered: false` and `clock_stepped: true`, and the `link` gap's `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt of the last current heartbeat before the reset; controls: a node that lets the chain close the stepped `node-start` gap as covered, which writes `covered: true`, and a node that caps the floor at no point and keeps it, at an unwatched close, as the later of its value and that close's `to_utc`, which holds W and R at the saved moment and starts the `link` gap there, about three and a half minutes after that receipt; **seventh case, W and R capped** (reworked for D6's N2 on #641 at `02773aa1`): heartbeats current, then the node and the sensor stop together for one minute, the sensor database kept, and the node's clock is stepped back five minutes during the stop; the connection is reset 2 s after the node's receipt of the heartbeat that closes the `node-start` gap, unwatched, before the next heartbeat: the `link` gap's `from_utc` is no later than that receipt; control: a node that caps the floor but caps W and R at no point, which the close does not raise because they are later than its end, and which starts the gap at the saved moment, about four minutes after that receipt (a node that caps R alone passes, as SL-16 allows); **eighth case, a step between two ticks:** heartbeats current every 10 s with `packets` rising, the node's wall clock stepped back five minutes 3 s after a heartbeat's receipt, and the connection reset 5 s after the step, the test clock running no node tick and the source sending no heartbeat between the step and the reset: `from_utc` is no later than that heartbeat's receipt on the new clock, and `time.node-stepped` is written once; controls: a node that runs the step check only at its tick, which starts the gap at W on the old clock, about five minutes late, and a node that moves W and R to the wall-clock time at the gap's open, and not back by the step, which starts it 8 s after that receipt; **ninth case, E's samples** (SL-16 at #641's `bb7f0d91`): ticks on time every 10 s; the test source raises the delay of its heartbeats by 10 s a heartbeat to 120 s and holds it there, `packets` rising, as in `TestGapHeartbeatSilence`'s fifth case; then it stops making heartbeats and keeps sending those it holds; the node's wall clock is stepped back 60 s, 1 s after a tick's receipt and 2 s before the receipt of the last heartbeat the source holds, with no tick between: the `silent` gap's `from_utc` is no later than that heartbeat's `at_utc` + E on the new clock; control: a node that moves W, R, the floor and the copy back by the step but not E's samples, which places W and R at that heartbeat's `at_utc` + E on the old clock and starts the gap about 60 s late; **tenth case, the cap waits for the `node-start` gap's close** (SL-16 at #641's `bb7f0d91`): heartbeats current; the node and the sensor stop together for one minute, the sensor database kept, and the node's clock is stepped back five minutes during the stop; the node starts with the sensor still stopped, the test clock runs a node tick and a cursor save 10 s after the start, and the node is killed 20 s after the start, before the `node-start` gap is written `open`, then started again with the sensor running: the `node-start` gap after the second start has its `from_utc` at the moment saved before the first stop, with `clock_stepped: true`; control: a node that caps W, R and the floor at the start, or at its first tick, and saves them, which starts that gap at the first start's wall-clock time, with no `clock_stepped`, and leaves the first stop in no gap; **eleventh case, the step check's threshold** (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D6's N3 on #641 at `6723bf90`): a ten-minute link cut in one run with a full chain, the node's wall clock running slow against its monotonic clock by 0.5 ms each second throughout, as a slew does, 300 ms over the cut: the gap closes as covered, with no `clock_stepped` and no `time.node-stepped`, at any interval between the node's checks, since the wall clock never runs backward and no two checks see it fall more than 300 ms behind (D6's N2 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`); control: a node that acts on any backward difference, which marks the gap at its first check in the cut and closes it unwatched; and the same cut with one step back of 3 s five minutes into it and no slew: the gap closes with `covered: false` and `clock_stepped: true`; control: a node that acts only on a step of a minute or more, which closes it as covered);
  - `TestGapHeldInW` (leg 14: the gap's event refused past the ceiling, then a restart: the gap is written from the earlier of W and R; the same with `sensorrun.json` deleted before the start: the same; control: W moved at the gap's open, which loses it; **second case, a crash between an unwatched close and the save of the floor** (D6's B1 on #641 at `8f78d057`): a `silent` gap from R0 closes unwatched at t1 with heartbeats current after it, and the node is killed at each write point from that close to the first cursor save after it, then started with the sensor running: where the closed gap is in the health stream, at least once, the `node-start` gap's `from_utc` is no earlier than t1, and at every kill point no moment from R0 to t1 is outside a gap in the stream; control: a node that commits the closing event first and saves the raise with the next save, which, killed between them, starts the `node-start` gap from R0; second control (D5's, for the mechanism in "Where a gap starts" above): a node that saves the raise with no gap held for the floor, which, killed between that save and the event's commit, leaves R0 to t1 in no gap; **third case, the copy while a gap is held for the floor** (SL-16 at #641's `02773aa1` and `bb7f0d91`; D6's N2 on #641 at `cebfff61` and N1 at `02773aa1`): the same close, the leg making the node write `sensorrun.json` at one of two points, between the raise and its save, and between the raise's save and the event's commit, the node killed before the commit, and the sensor database deleted before the start: at each point the `node-start` gap's `from_utc` is no later than R0; controls: a node whose copy is the earlier of the raised W and R alone, which starts the gap at t1 at both points, with R0 to t1 in no gap, and a node that holds the copy back only from the raise's save, which starts it at t1 at the first point; the same, written at the second point, with the node's wall clock stepped back by more than the time since R0 between the raise's save and the file's write (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D6's N2 on #641 at `6723bf90`): the `node-start` gap's `from_utc` is no later than R0 moved back by the step; control: a node that takes the copy at each write as the earliest of W, R and the held `from_utc` as it holds them, with no hold on its rising, which writes W or R on the new clock, later than R0 on a common clock, and starts the gap there; **fourth case, a merge into the held gap** (SL-16 at #641's `bb7f0d91`; D6's N4 on #641 at `02773aa1`): the health class held past its ceiling from before t1 until after the `link` gap below closes; a `silent` gap from R0 closes unwatched at t1, as in `TestGapHeartbeatSilence`'s sixth case, so it is held for the floor; heartbeats current after it, `packets` rising; a second `silent` gap opens at S2 and closes unwatched at t2 in the same way while the first gap's event waits; one heartbeat after t2 the connection is reset and then restored with a full chain: once room returns, one record holds both gaps, from R0 to t2, `covered: false`, `merged` 2, and the `link` gap's `from_utc` is no earlier than t2; control: a node that holds the second gap in W with its raise waiting until the field clears, which starts the `link` gap at the earlier of W and R, no later than S2, inside the second gap; **fifth case, the mark through a merge** (D6's N1 on #641 at `6723bf90`): the fourth case, with the node's wall clock stepped back five minutes between t1 and S2, found at a node tick while the first gap is held and no gap is open: the merged record carries `clock_stepped: true`, and `time.node-stepped` is written once; control: a node that marks at a step only the gaps open then, which writes the merged record with no mark; **sixth case, the order and the pairing** (D6's B3 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`): the fourth case, with the leg refusing, by a seam, only the first gap's closing event from t1, and admitting every other record; after t2, while the field is still held, the node's wall clock stepped back by more than the time since t2 and less than the time since R0, which marks the held gap; the connection then reset and never restored, so a `link` gap C opens at the earlier of W and R on the new clock, by number after R0 and before t2; then the seam lets the first event through: the second gap's `open` record is never written, the merged record (R0 to t2, `merged` 2, `covered: false`, `clock_stepped: true`) is written before any record of C, and C's `open` record, written after it, is left unpaired by it; control: a node that writes a later gap's record while the held gap's event waits, which writes the second gap's `open` record and C's before the merged record, so that read by interval the merged record pairs both and C reads as closed while it stands, and read by an equal `from_utc` the second gap's record is left unpaired; **seventh case, a step while the second gap is open** (D6's N1 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`): the fourth case, with the node's wall clock stepped back five minutes between S2 and t2, found at a node tick while the second gap is open and the first is held: the merged record carries `clock_stepped: true`, and `time.node-stepped` is written once; control: a node that marks at a step only the gaps open then, and keeps at a merge only the held gap's mark, which writes the merged record with no mark (a node that lacks only the second passes, since the step rule marks the held gap whenever it marks a gap open while that gap is held); **eighth case, two `open` records of one `from_utc` across a node stop** (D6's N4 on #641 at `50a67730`): a `link` gap written `open` and still open when the node stops for one minute, the sensor running; at the start the `node-start` gap opens from the same earlier of W and R and is written `open`; it closes at the first current heartbeat that shows capture: its `closed` record pairs both `open` records, and no `open` record of the leg is left unpaired; control: a reader that pairs a `closed` record with one `open` record only, which leaves the `link` gap reading as open for good).
  - **Gaps that never close, and the node's own causes:** C8-7's legs 10 to 13 at `41a073f2` (D6's B1 on #641, comment 6837825426). D3 numbers them and leaves their names to D4 and D5; these are D5's:
    - `TestGapNeverBackOpen` (leg 10, never back: the link cut and never restored: within 60 s plus one node tick a `link` gap is in the health stream as `open`, and it stays open; control: a node that writes a gap only when it closes, which has nothing in the stream; **second case, as replaced** (D6's B3 on #641 at `467c1dd6`): the test source sends no heartbeat after the last current one, keeps sending ticks, and resets the connection 20 s after that heartbeat's receipt, before the ninth cause falls due: `from_utc` is no later than that receipt, cause `link`; control: a node with the ninth cause intact that opens `link` at the close, which starts it 20 s late. The case it replaces, the link made to drop every packet with no FIN or RST, had no working control: the ninth cause falls due at W + 30 s, no later than the idle close, so a node that opened `link` at the close still wrote the gap from W (SL-16's note at #641's `da7a84ee`)); **third case, a gap that never closes after a flat stretch** (D6's B1 on #641 at `da7a84ee`): every interface's `packets` flat, carrier `up`, from four minutes before W, then the connection reset at W and never restored: the `link` gap is in the health stream as `open` within 60 s plus one node tick of W, with `from_utc` no later than R; control: a node that moves a gap's start to R only when the gap closes unwatched, D6's fix as worded, which writes it `open` from W, four minutes late, and never closes it; **fourth case, the `open` record after a step back during a stop** (SL-16 at #641's `c0cf1ecc`; D6's B1 on #641 at `6723bf90`): the node and the sensor stop together for one minute, the sensor database kept, the node's clock is stepped back five minutes during the stop, and the sensor never comes back: within 60 s plus one node tick of the start, the `node-start` gap is in the health stream as `open`, from the saved moment, with `clock_stepped: true`; controls: a node that counts the 60 s from W on the wall clock alone, which writes the record about 300 s after the start, and a node that marks the gap only at the cap, at its close, which writes it with no `clock_stepped`;
    - `TestGapHeldOpen` (leg 11, held: a fork hold for ten minutes, then an enrolment with a new key: a `held` gap is written `open` at the hold, with cause `held`, and the new key's run does not close it as covered: it closes at that run's first current heartbeat with `covered: false`; and the same with the dial limit spent: the link never comes up, and the gap stays open; controls: for the fork, the hold's close recorded as `link`, which fails the leg's cause; for the dial limit, a node that opens a gap only when a connection ends, which opens none at a hold made between connections; and for the close, a node that takes any well-spaced run of heartbeats after the gap as its chain, without requiring the run of W's heartbeat, which closes the gap as covered on the new key's heartbeats; **20 s case** (D6's B3 on #641 at `467c1dd6`): heartbeats withheld after the last current one, ticks continuing, and the fork's second connection 20 s after W's receipt: `from_utc` is no later than that receipt; control: a node that opens the gap at its decision, which starts it 20 s late);
    - `TestGapRevokedOpen` (leg 12, revoked: a revocation closes the link: a `revoked` gap is written `open`, and with no new enrolment it stays open and is never closed; then an enrolment with a new key: the gap closes at that key's first current heartbeat with `covered: false`; controls: the close recorded as `link`, or not recorded at all; and a node that closes the gap as covered on the new key's run; **20 s case:** the same withheld heartbeats, and the revocation applied 20 s after W's receipt: `from_utc` is no later than that receipt; control: a node that opens the gap at its decision, which starts it 20 s late);
    - `TestGapSensorFailedOpen` (leg 13, `sensor-failed`: a recovered panic stops the sensor's work: a `sensor-failed` gap is written `open` at the panic, not at the next start; control: a node whose only trace is the `node-start` reach-back at its next start; **20 s case:** the same withheld heartbeats, and the panic 20 s after W's receipt: `from_utc` is no later than that receipt; control: a node that opens the gap at the panic, which starts it 20 s late).
    - `TestGapNeverWatchedFromEnrolment` (leg 15, at `9d74bb73`: a sensor enrolled while the node runs, whose first connection is accepted at once and whose backlog takes 90 s: a `link` gap from `enrolled_at` is in the health stream as `open` within 60 s plus one node tick, and closes with `covered: false` at the first current heartbeat; the same with the first current heartbeat 5 s after the enrolment: one `closed` record with `covered: false` from `enrolled_at`; control: a node that opens a gap only when a connection ends or a dial fails, which writes nothing in either case; **second case, no R** (D6's B1 on #641 at `8f78d057`): the first connection accepted with no backlog, and the first run flat from its first heartbeat, every carrier `up`: the never-watched gap closes unwatched at that heartbeat, and the `blind` gap that follows starts no later than the never-watched gap's `to_utc`; control: a node that holds no R, since that run has had no rise, which starts the `blind` gap at W, about `blind_after_s` late).
    - `TestGapHeartbeatSilence` (leg 16, the ninth cause: the link up, accepted ticks every 10 s, no heartbeat for five minutes, then heartbeats again in the same run with no declared loss: a `silent` gap from W is in the health stream as `open` within 60 s plus one node tick of W, and closes with `covered: false`; **second case:** heartbeats made every 10 s and delivered four minutes late behind records: the gap closes as covered once the chain is read; controls: a node that moves W to the next current heartbeat whatever the silence before it, which writes nothing in the first case; and a node that closes this cause only as unwatched, which writes `covered: false` in the second; **third case** (D6's N1 on #641 at `467c1dd6`): heartbeats made every 10 s and delivered two minutes late, inside `late_bound_s`, then on time: the gap closes as covered at the first heartbeat whose lateness is under `HeartbeatGapMax`; control: a node that ends this cause at the first current heartbeat, which judges a chain of two where the fourth part asks for four, and writes `covered: false`; **fourth case, a flat stretch across the stall** (D6's B2 at `467c1dd6`): every interface's `packets` flat from four minutes before W, heartbeats silent from W for two minutes, then resuming still flat: `blind` joins the gap, whose `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt of the last heartbeat with a rise, where R lies or earlier (R is placed as W is); control: a gap that keeps W as its start, which begins four minutes late; **fifth case, heartbeats late and then stopped** (D6's B2 on #641 at `da7a84ee`): ticks on time; the test source raises the delay of its heartbeats by 10 s a heartbeat to 120 s and holds it there, `packets` rising, so every heartbeat stays current and the ninth cause does not fall due; then it stops making heartbeats while it keeps sending those it holds, and ticks: the `silent` gap opens once 30 s have passed since the last heartbeat's receipt, with `from_utc` no later than that heartbeat's `at_utc` + E, and it is written `open` when it opens, since by then it has stood 60 s from W: the test clock runs a node tick 45 s after that receipt, and none other between 30 s and 60 s after it, and the gap is in the health stream as `open` by that tick (D6's N1 on #641 at `cebfff61`); controls (D6's N1 on #641 at `8f78d057`): a node that places W and R at their receipts, which starts the gap about 120 s late, and a node that bounds R but places W at its receipt, which passes `from_utc`, since R is W's heartbeat while the counts rise, and writes the gap `open` only once it has stood 60 s from that receipt, which is after the tick at 45 s; **sixth case, R carried past an unwatched close** (D6's B1 on #641 at `8f78d057`): W and R at R0, counts rising; no heartbeat for two minutes, so a `silent` gap opens from R0; heartbeats resume 40 s late with counts rising, then arrive on time with counts flat, and the gap closes unwatched at t1, since the chain fails its third part; 90 s later, in the same run, the connection is reset for two minutes, the backlog's counts rising, and restored with a full chain: the `link` gap closes as covered, with `from_utc` no earlier than t1; control: a node that keeps R from before the `silent` gap, which writes the covered gap from R0; **seventh case, no move back below the floor:** the same stall, but the heartbeats after it arrive 25 s late and stay so, counts rising, so the gap closes unwatched at t1, the receipt of the first of them; one heartbeat later the connection is reset for two minutes and restored with a full chain: the `link` gap's `from_utc` is no earlier than t1; control: a node that raises W and R at the close but lets the next heartbeat's placement, its `at_utc` + E about 15 s before t1, take them below it, which starts the `link` gap there; **eighth case, a step found at the close** (D6's B1 on #641 at `02773aa1`): the seventh case, with the node's wall clock stepped back 60 s, 5 s before t1, and no node tick between the step and t1, the receipt on the new clock: the `silent` gap's record carries `clock_stepped: true`, `time.node-stepped` is written once, and the `link` gap's `from_utc` is no earlier than t1; control: a node that runs the step check only at its tick, at placements and at opens, which writes the `silent` gap without `clock_stepped` and starts the `link` gap about 15 s before t1).
    - `TestGapFlatCountsCarrierUp` (leg 17: a ten-minute link cut, one run, a heartbeat every 10 s, every interface's carrier `up` and its `packets` flat for the whole cut: the gap closes with `covered: false`, and `sensor.past-blind` is written for the same stretch; control: a chain that checks the carrier alone, which closes it as covered. **Second case, the `blind` close** (D6's N3 on #641): the link current, every interface's `packets` flat for `blind_after_s` and then rising again: a `blind` gap is written `open` from no later than the node's receipt of the last heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose (R, placed as W is; D6's N3 on #641 at `8f78d057`), and closes with `covered: false` at the first current heartbeat in which one rises; controls: a `blind` gap closed as covered by the chain, and one opened when the node finds every interface blind, which starts `blind_after_s` late. **Cases three to seven** (D6's B1 and B2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`; gap leg 17 at `da7a84ee`). The third to sixth are each a ten-minute link cut from W, in one run, with a heartbeat every 10 s and the backlog through, and each gap closes with `covered: false`: **third,** every carrier `down` for 60 s inside the cut, `packets` rising before and after (control: a chain judged on stretches as long as `blind_after_s`, which closes it as covered); **fourth,** every interface's `packets` flat for seven minutes inside the cut, carrier `up` (control: a chain that reads "blind" as CH-31's windowed state over a stretch, which needs ten minutes flat and closes it as covered); **fifth,** every interface flat from four minutes before W, the node alone restarted inside the cut, and flat until two minutes after W, and `from_utc` no later than R (controls: a node that counts from the chain's first heartbeat, which closes it as covered, and a gap that keeps W as its start, which begins four minutes late); **sixth,** the same flat stretch with no restart, `from_utc` no later than R (control: a gap from W, which starts four minutes late); **seventh,** the cables pulled 3 s after W's heartbeat, the link current, the next heartbeat showing `packets` risen, from frames before the pull, and every carrier `down`: a `blind` gap opens with `from_utc` no later than W (control: a node that starts a `blind` gap at the last heartbeat with a rise, whatever its carrier, and not at the earlier of that and W, which starts it a heartbeat late); **eighth, the sixth case's flat stretch with a new run inside the cut** (D6's B1 on #641 at `da7a84ee`): every interface flat, carrier `up`, from R = W − 240 s; the connection reset at W; the old run stays flat until it ends at W + 180 s, and a new run begins, whose counts rise; the old run's heartbeats arrive as a backlog before the new run's hello, since the old run is flushed first and no record of an older run follows a newer run's hello (CH-16, SL-14; D6's N2 on #641 at `8f78d057`), and show 420 s flat; the gap opens as `link` from no later than R, becomes `restart` at the new run's hello, and closes with `covered: false`, `from_utc` no later than R (control: a node that moves a gap's start to R only when `blind` joins it or a chain within one run fails, `da7a84ee`'s rule, which writes it from W, four minutes late); **ninth, R with heartbeats late** (D6's B2 on #641 at `da7a84ee`): ticks on time; the test source raises the delay of its heartbeats by 10 s a heartbeat to 120 s and holds it there, the link current; every carrier goes `down` 3 s after a heartbeat made at h, whose `packets` rose: the `blind` gap opens with `from_utc` no later than h's `at_utc` + E (control: a node that places R and W at their receipts, which starts it about 120 s late); the same with every `packets` flat, carrier `up`, from the heartbeat made at h until the carriers go `down` 60 s later: `from_utc` is no later than h's `at_utc` + E (control: a node that bounds W but places R at its receipt, which starts it at W, about 60 s late)).
    - `TestGapLostWrittenOnce` (a declared span admitted: one `sensor.coverage-gap` record with cause `lost`, `state` `closed`, `covered: false` and the span's times, and no `open` record; control: the first text's two records, `open` then `closed`).
- **Tests of the ceiling:** SL-T13 control 1 (the ceiling removed: the class fills, `collection.missed` is refused and the entry's runs are held); the outage leg (the forward target down for `H` hours on the test clock, with the ceiling in place: no run is held); `TestPlatformFloorShortNodeStarts` (a quota below `C + S`: the node starts and stores a run, the sensor is not started, and the alarm stands; control: a load refusal); `TestCoverageGapNotBehindPool` (the pool spent: a gap is still written; control: one shared pool); `TestLostGapNotBehindOtherGaps` (the other gaps' budget spent by drops: a `lost` gap is still written; control: one budget for every cause); `TestHeldEventsSurviveRestart` (control: held in memory only); `TestPoolPerNameCap` (C13's K-5: `sensor.counters` due every minute for an hour gives at most 4 records, none sooner than 10 minutes after the last, every rise merged into the next; another pool name due 10 times in the hour gives at most 4, merged; controls: no per-name cap, under which one name spends the pool's 24, and the 10 minutes not kept); `TestPoolWaitOrder` (C13's K-6: the pool spent, then a `sensor.counters`, a `sensor.run-loss`, an alarm's event and a `sensor.redial-granted` due together; with room for one, `sensor.redial-granted` is written and the rest wait; with room for three, only `sensor.counters` waits; each waiting event is held in `sensorrun.json`, counted in `health_held`, and written first when room returns; a coverage gap due meanwhile is written under its own budget; controls: a pool that writes in arrival order, and a gap held behind the pool).

### 6. The audit class (F8)

- **`sensor.link_refused`:** D3's cap, **at most once in a stretch and once an hour for each sensor, generation and reason** (C8-5; CH-11, CH-45; ruling 2 on #631 (comment 6804993239); D5's B5 on #634). **A stretch ends at the next accepted hello from that sensor, and at a node start:** the stretch is kept in memory, and the hour, which is durable, still bounds what restarts can add. So an `expired` sensor, which never gets an accepted hello, writes one record for each node start, at most one an hour, and not 24 a day. At most 6 × 2 × 24 = 288 records a day at the list's maximum, since a sensor has one live generation at a time, and only under accepted hellos or restarts in between. **The cap's hour starts again at each succeeded `sensor.enrolled` of that sensor,** because the generation changes; only an admin-signed enrolment changes it, so what a sensor can drive is unchanged. **The fold derives a fork hold only from a `run-forked` record of the enrolment's own generation,** which the record carries (C8-4, C8-5). Within a run the cap runs on the monotonic clock. Across a restart it starts from `sensorrun.json`'s last record time for each sensor, generation and reason, written with each record, folded forward from the audit records after `sensors.json`'s `through_seq`. It does not rely on the audit read at start that runs only with event baselines (`newEventRun`, `eventbaselines_run.go`). An unreadable file, a file that is lost or behind (§1.1), or a fold that fails, holds the cap from the start and raises `sensor-state-unread` (cause `run-file`, or `fold-read`); the start goes on. A time after now is taken as now.
- **SL-10's case** (D3's note 2): a record under another `sensor_id` is refused (`sensor-id`), raises `sensor-identity` once a stretch, and writes `sensor.link_refused` (`sensor-id`) under this cap, with the connection left open. **A tick under another `sensor_id` closes the connection as `tick`** (CH-8, CH-11; D4's note 2 on #633), and writes `sensor.link_refused` (`sensor-id`) under the same cap (D3's note 8 on #627).
- **The `node-sensors` cursor** holds the audit stream through a read error, and moves on only for an unparseable record or past three quarters of the audit class's quota, after writing the revoked pins it has read (§1.3).
- Other audit records a sensor's work writes are caused by an administrator (`sensor.enrolled`, `sensor.revoked`, `policy.apply`), or are mode changes under item 1's cap.
- **Tests:** `TestLinkRefusedOncePerStretch` (an `expired` sensor dialled for two hours with no node restart: one record; with a node restart each hour: one record an hour; control: the hour cap alone, which writes one an hour with no restart); SL-T13's leg (d), with **two legs for the cap** (C8-5): with `sensorrun.json` unreadable at a start, the node starts, stores a run, and writes no `sensor.link_refused` inside the first hour (control: a file read that fails the start); and with a last record time in the future, the node reads it as now and writes that reason's next record one hour later (control: the future time taken as it stands, which silences the reason until then). And SL-T9 (l), a second fork after a new enrolment inside the hour, which writes its own `run-forked` record (control: a cap keyed without the generation, under which the node dials after a restart). **And `TestForkHoldOwnGeneration`** (ruling 6 on #633 and #634 (comment 6805936000); D6's B2 on #633): a fork at generation g with the write of the held state made to fail, so that only the `run-forked` record remains; a `rekey` to g + 1; a node restart. Generation g + 1 is dialled, within SL-30 (control: a fold that takes a hold from any generation's `run-forked` record, which holds g + 1).

### 7. Faults stay in the sensor's database

The link's commits and position saves go to the sensor's database only. What else a sensor's work writes, each bounded: the hold's records and the detector's checkpoints in the configuration class, under item 3's ceiling; health events under item 5; audit records under item 6; `sensors.json` and `sensorrun.json` in the store directory.

### 8. The tests that show it

**In process, on the test clock** (a new package, `platform/node/sensortest`, not `platform/node`, whose Windows run is near its 720 s warning, D4-44; `platform/node` gains the wiring and one start test): every test named in items 1 to 6, and SL-T13's legs (d) and (e), which need hours of test clock and so cannot run over a real link.

**On the rig, in real time, over the real link** (P4-4-R1, `CI-Linux`), with SER collection against the simulator on a schedule, Syslog over TCP and UDP, and small platform and audit quotas:

| Leg | What the sensor does | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| (a) flood | Observations at the link's full rate, resolved and unresolved, for twice the time the observation quota takes to fill, with GT-PCAP's labelled case in the middle | Rig |
| (b) cut and stall | The connection killed every 5 s for 10 minutes; then a peer at the enrolled address that accepts TCP and never completes TLS | Rig |
| (c) blind | Heartbeats with flat counters, on one interface and on all | Rig |
| (d) audit (D3) | Accepted and refused hellos in turn, under every reason it can cause, for more than an hour of test clock, with the node restarted repeatedly | In process |
| (e) gap flapping | One heartbeat, then 61 s of silence, for two hours of test clock | In process |
| (f) skew | A `sensor_utc` offset of 10 s across 20 scheduled runs | Rig |
| (g) two sensors | A floods, B is normal (when a second is built) | Rig |
| (h) clean close loop (D3's L1) | A source that closes cleanly after every hello | Rig |

**Pass conditions, absolute** (D6's B7, in place of the first text's 10 %): every scheduled run is sent and stores its rows; no `collection.missed` and no collection alarm; Syslog received equals sent within the listener's own limits; **the longest wait of any scheduler call on the link's lock is under 2 s,** measured by the node and shown in status; `dispatch_timeouts` and `result_timeouts` stay 0; the sensor's health events stay below the ceiling; `sensor.link_refused` stays within the cap; in (a), the labelled case's alert is stored, with its latency reported; in (h), the dials stay within 10 a minute; in (g), B's commits are unchanged by A.

**Controls,** each of which must make a leg fail: (1) the ceiling removed; (2) the fairness rule removed, with a seam that slows the network commit; (3) the time cap removed, or started afresh at each start; (4) D3's: the observation class's quota check removed, so the flood takes the volume; (5) the redial floor removed, for leg (h).

### 9. A fault in the sensor's code is contained (D6's item 4)

- **What `main` has** (`desk`): no `recover` in `platform/node`, `platform/sensorlink` or `cmd/otsec`, so a panic in the sensor's work ends the node, and with it SER and Syslog collection. If it comes from a stored observation, the detector reads it again after each restart.
- **Requirement, not built.** The sensor manager's goroutine, each connection's read loop, and the `sensorlink` calls the scheduler makes (`Dispatch`, `Result`, `Abandon`) each `recover`.
  - In the manager or a read loop, a recovered panic raises `sensor-failed`, naming the sensor, and **stops that sensor's work until a restart:** its connection is closed, nothing is dialled and the detector is not driven. The node and every other stream go on.
  - Before each detector step the manager notes the observation's seq in memory. After a panic there, it writes that seq to `sensorrun.json` as the sensor's poison record. At the next start the detector counts that observation as refused and passes it, so a stored observation cannot crash the sensor's work at every start. A run file that is lost or behind (§1.1) can lose the poison record; the panic then recurs once at the next start, is contained the same way, and the record is written again.
  - In a scheduler call, a recovered panic is taken as a timeout (item 4): the run goes on, untied, with its session kept and marked. **This widens the `hold-timeout` key beyond CH-47 on `main`,** which defines it as a tie that a timed-out wait on the link's lock prevented: here it also covers a tie that a recovered panic prevented. CH-47 says so (D4's note on #633).
  - **Limit:** `recover` does not catch a fatal runtime error (memory exhausted, a concurrent map write). Those still end the process, and the service manager restarts it after 60 s.
- **Test:** `TestSensorPanicContained` (a seam that panics in the detector's drive: a run is still sent and stored, `sensor-failed` stands, and after a restart the poison record is passed; control: no `recover`, which ends the node); `TestSchedulerPanicTakenAsTimeout` (C13's T-12: a seam that panics in `Result` while the session's tie is made: the panic is recovered, `collection.run` carries `hold: timeout`, the session is judged and its own-session alerts come under `hold-timeout`, `result_timeouts` rises and `network-session-not-tied` is raised, `sensor-failed` is not raised and no `sensor-failed` gap opens, and the run, the sensor's work and the node go on; controls: a node that drops the session on a recovered panic, which raises no alert, and no `recover`, which ends the node). **As D1 ruled, and as C13's CH-47 and T-12 state it at #645's `1c454cb6`:** a scheduler call's panic is taken as a timeout, raises no `sensor-failed`, does not stop the sensor's work and opens no `sensor-failed` gap; the session is still judged, and `network-session-not-tied` keeps the fault visible. *Third control:* a node that raises `sensor-failed` for it, or stops the sensor's work, which opens a `sensor-failed` gap.

### 10. The node's stop (D6's item 4)

- **The order:** `Runtime.Close` first stops the sensor manager, then closes `Runtime.dbs` as today.
- **The bound:** the manager gets `SensorStopTimeout` = 10 s. It cancels its context, which every store call it makes takes, so a `Resume` or a prune stops at its next step. It writes the final drop counts and the position within that time if it can, and closes every handle that no caller holds.
- **Past 10 s,** `Close` goes on without it: a sensor database still in a call is left to the process's exit, which releases its lock, and the node's own databases close as today. So a sensor database stuck in a `Resume` never holds the node's stop, and the local stop of §1.3 stays a restart.
- **Test:** `TestCloseWithStuckSensorResume` (a sensor `Resume` that never returns: `Close` returns within 10 s and the evidence database is closed cleanly; control: an unbounded wait for the manager).

### 11. The link key (F11)

- **What it closes:** F11 (§1.0), the coupling that this design's first text made by putting the link key in the node key register.
