The register is all or nothing (`modules/security/nodekey/register.go`, `Parse`, `Validate`), and the node resolves it at its start for every target and listener (`NewForwarding`, `NewListening`).
- **Requirement, not built:** the link key is in a file of its own beside the register (§1.1, NK-1 to NK-6). **The sensor manager reads it after `Start` returns; nothing in `Start`, `NewForwarding` or `NewListening` reads it,** and the register's reader never sees it. Any fault in it holds back the sensor links only, with `sensor-link-key` standing and the node started and collecting: the file missing while a sensor is configured, unreadable or malformed; an entry that fails its checks; a provider that does not have the key; NK-2's clash with a register key; and `key_register` set but not loaded. An enrolment that arrives meanwhile stays pending (§1.3).
- **Tests,** each with its control:
  - `TestLinkKeyFaultNodeStarts`: with the file missing, unreadable, malformed, or naming a key the provider does not have, the node starts, stores a run and raises the alarm, and no SYN goes to the sensor. *Control:* the file read inside `Start`, which fails the start.
  - `TestLinkKeyNotInRegister`: `LoadCertificate` given the link key's reference finds no entry, and a register holding an entry of kind `link-key` is refused whole, as today. *Control:* the kind added to the register's `validate`, which loads it.
  - `TestLinkKeyClash` (NK-2): a link key whose public key equals a `tls-key` entry's: no sensor is dialled, the alarm stands, and the `tls-key` still serves its listener; and with `key_register` set and not loaded, no dial and the alarm. *Control:* a reader with no clash check, which dials with the shared key.
  - NK-3 and NK-4: no certificate for the link key is in the file, the register or the store directory after a start; the chain holds one `policy.apply` for the entry; status shows the pin the test's listener saw in the handshake. *Controls:* a node that writes its handshake certificate to disk; a status that shows a pin from the configuration.
  - NK-6: the register's goldens and schema test are unchanged, and a node built without the sensor manager starts and stores a run with a link-key file beside its register. *Control:* a `link-key` kind added to the register, which changes the goldens.

## 1.10 Bounds set in this note

| Bound | Value | Why | Past it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TCP connect, write | 5 s, 10 s | Node-local | Closed, counted, back-off |
| Handshake, hello, idle | 10 s, 10 s, 30 s | C13 0.2 (CH-4) | Closed, counted, back-off |
| Back-off | 1 s to 60 s, ±20 % | Bounded redial | — |
| `RedialFloor` | 10 s between immediate redials after `run-complete`, only when the node saw the run finished | CH-38; recorded ruling 2 (#628, comment 6803459738) | Back-off |
| `MaxSensors` | 2 in the shape, 1 built | Ruling 1 | A third entry: load refused (shape). A second before it is built: not started, alarm |
| Retired sensors | at most `MaxSensors` | §1.2 | Load refused (shape) |
| Skew | 2,000 ms raise, 1,500 ms clear on E; no tie while the latest sample is outside 2,000 ms | §1.5 | Alarm, no ties, aggregated alerts |
| `max_skew_ms` | 2,000 ms, fixed | CH-22 | — |
| `TickMinSpacing` | 5 s | CH-8 | Counted under `tick_early`, no further work |
| `MaxLostSpans` | 8 spans in a `lost` list and in `node.lost` | CH-40 | The two nearest spans merged by the sensor |
| Aggregated alerts | 8 assets named, the rest counted | Ruling 13 | — |
| `backlog_tie_s` | 60 s by default, at most 120 s, on the node's clock | CH-39; recorded ruling 2 (#628, comment 6803459738) | No tie for the backlog; at or below it, the `made_utc` check |
| Late index | 15 minutes, 1,024 entries | §1.6, unmeasured | Untied, counted, alarm |
| `late_bound_s` | 180 s, 30 s to 3,600 s | CH-23; recorded ruling 2 (#628, comment 6803459738) | `node.late`, counted |
| Sensor buffer | `outage_hours` at the site's rate, as a sizing rule | Difference 9 on #627 | `sensor-buffer-filling` at 3/4 |
| `ack` | within 1 s of T moving, and at least every 10 s | C13 0.2 (CH-9) | — |
| `MaxLinkCursorBytes` | 2,048, for a text form of at most 1,934 bytes (1,575 before what is held with W; 192 before P4-4-L6) | §1.4; CH-18 | — |
| `HeartbeatGapMax` | 30 s between consecutive heartbeats of a chain, by their `at_utc`, its ends included; and at least the gap's length on the node's clock ÷ 30 s heartbeats in the chain. Three 10 s `SensorTickInterval`s, so it depends on the 0.2 heartbeat cadence that CH-39 states | §1.9 item 5; CH-39, CH-45; SL-16 | The gap is not closed as covered |
| `HoldLockWait` | 2 s, the scheduler first | §1.9 item 4, unmeasured | Session kept and marked; alarm |
| Detector drive | about 1 a second; 1,000 observations or 200 ms a drive | §1.9 item 3, unmeasured | Next drive |
| Detector checkpoint | at most once every 60 s | Ruling 10 | — |
| Detector lag | 60 s raise, 30 s clear | §1.9 item 3 | `network-detector-lag` |
| `blind_after_s` | 300 s by default, 60 s to 3,600 s | Difference 6 on #627, an assumption | Alarm; a gap when all are blind |
| Coverage unknown | 60 s of accepted ticks with no heartbeat that is not late | §1.8, CH-44 | `sensor-no-heartbeat` |
| Sensor buffer floor | 256 MiB, as a hello states `buffer_bytes` | CH-13 row 9, CH-20 | `sensor-buffer-small`, standing |
| Health record | 4,096 bytes stored | §1.9 item 5, unmeasured | — |
| Sensor health events | 45 an hour: 12 drop-counts; 4 `lost` coverage gaps and 4 coverage gaps of every other cause, each on its own budget; a pool of 24 others, each name at most 4 an hour and `sensor.counters` no sooner than 10 minutes after its last; mode changes 1 an hour for each database. 3 for a retired sensor | §1.9 item 5; CH-45 | Waiting order stated; merged, carried; a `lost` gap merged only with `lost` gaps |
| Platform floor and ceiling | `C + S` checked at load; `quota − C` at run time | Ruling 9; recorded ruling 1 (#628, comment 6803459738) | Below the floor: the sensor not started, alarm. Past the ceiling: events held |
| `SensorStopTimeout` | 10 s | §1.9 item 10 | `Close` goes on without the manager |
| `sensor.link_refused` | once in a stretch and once an hour for each sensor, generation and reason; a stretch ends at an accepted hello or a node start; the hour restarts at each succeeded `sensor.enrolled` | C8-5; ruling 2 on #631; CH-11, CH-45 | Counted; the next record's `count` |
| SL-30's dial limit | three dials for each address and pin until a hello is accepted from it and saved (CH-13 rows 5b and 6); each at most 5 s | SL-30; ruling 5 on #631 | `sensor-dial-limit`, standing, until an administrator's re-arm |
| `frames_outgoing` | any non-zero value in a heartbeat taken with no baseline held for the run (its first, or the first after an eviction or a lost database), or any rise against the baseline; late heartbeats included | Ruling 3 on #633 and #634; D1's ruling on D6's T1; CH-26; D5's B1 on #634 | `sensor-transmitted`, standing until an administrator clears it; raised by a run file lost, unreadable or behind |
| Expiry warning | 30 days, hourly | SL-8 | — |
| Revocation | fold to close within 1 s; signing to close about 15 s, about 615 s at worst, open under `collectorHold`; the fast path within 15 s | Rulings 2 and 3 | — |

## 1.11 What the node needs from other contracts and roles

**From C13 0.2 (D4, #627).** Status of D5's first asks, as ruled on #627:

| First ask | Status |
| --- | --- |
| 1. Hellos with "the oldest seq held" | **Superseded:** CH-7's `newest_seq` and `drops_channel`, which D3 and D5 accept |
| 2. The `ack` and its meaning | Met by CH-9 and CH-17, with ruling 2's durable totals |
| 3. A send time in each heartbeat | **Superseded:** the `tick` (CH-8, CH-21); the heartbeat's `at_utc` is its made time |
| 4. The capture interval of evicted records | Ruled into 0.2 (difference 3): the declared interval and `node.lost` |
| 5. Flows sent in capture order | Ruled into 0.2 and marked [S2] (difference 2), with the detector's fallback |
| 6. A capture-state signal | Met by CH-31's `carrier` |
| 7. The skew mark and key | Ruled into 0.2 (difference 4): `node.skewed` and the `clock-skew` key |
| 8. ALPN | Ruled: `otsec.netobs/0` (difference 8) |
| 9. PRP with two sensors | Met by CH-30 option (ii) |
| 10. Room for the cursor | `MaxLinkCursorBytes` is the node's constant (`records-auditor`), which CH-18 states: **2,048 bytes under P4-4-L6** (192 before); R-5 runs with 2,048 |
| 11. The configuration digest | Met by CH-32 |

**Shared names and figures under P4-4-L5.** D4 applies #631's 16 items to `docs/phase4/D4-c13-0.2-proposal.md` in #634. These amendments touch names or figures that the two documents share, checked against #634, and none changes a value already shared: the cap's key by the sensor, the generation and the reason (CH-1, CH-45; H-1, H-4 and T-5 asserting the audit record); a tick under another `sensor_id` writing `sensor.link_refused` (`sensor-id`) (CH-11, CH-8); SL-30's dial limit before an address has answered, and its standing alarm `sensor-dial-limit`, under the cap (CH-1, CH-38, CH-45); CH-13 row 5b's save on a `renew`'s first resume (H-7); `egress_check` and `sensor-egress-absent` (CH-27); `drops_holder` and `frames_overlong` as capture losses, and `frames_outgoing` with `sensor-transmitted` (CH-26, CH-45); `sensor-state-unread` for state or a position that cannot be read; and the one list of the pool's names (§1.9 item 5; CH-45). **The names are final** (ruling 4 on #633 and #634 (comment 6805936000)): `sensor-dial-limit`, `sensor-egress-absent`, `sensor-state-unread` and `sensor-transmitted`.

**Before P4-4-L5, this design and `docs/phase4/D4-c13-0.2-proposal.md` (#627) did not differ** on any name, figure or rule they share, checked against #627's §12 bounds table, CH-8, CH-22, CH-40 and CH-45 at that head; the tick spacing (`TickMinSpacing`), `max_skew_ms` and `MaxLostSpans` are listed in §1.2 and §1.10 for that reason. CH-45 took the coverage gaps' own budget and the total of 41 (45 under P4-4-L6); CH-40 and CH-18 took the spans kept in `sensorrun.json` (in the cursor under P4-4-L6), `spans_cut`, and the cut fixed once with later messages compared with the stated span. The cut's third term is the node's receipt time plus `max_skew_ms`, and an empty window writes no `node.lost` entry (CH-40 of `docs/phase4/D4-c13-0.2-proposal.md` (#627); recorded ruling 4 (#628, comment 6803459738)).

**Agreed with `docs/phase4/D4-c13-0.2-proposal.md` (#627), and used here:** the hellos, with `current` (CH-7); the break, the backlog per outage judged by its longest break, `backlog_tie_s` and the `made_utc + E` window (CH-39); E's sign (CH-22); `RedialFloor` 10 s (CH-38); `late_bound_s` 180 s (CH-23); `node.lost` as a list with its checks, `spans_cut`, the spans kept durably (in the cursor since P4-4-L6), the `lost` gap and `sensor-records-lost` (CH-40, CH-18); `prior` durable (in the cursor since P4-4-L6) (CH-16, CH-13 row 6); a `renew` with the pin unchanged carrying the position over, and the node's own count on `replace` (CH-12); `sensor-buffer-small` (CH-13 row 9, CH-20); the closes `enrolment` and `shutdown` (CH-11, CH-37); `sensor-no-heartbeat` and `sensor.past-blind` (CH-44); the three aggregate keys naming up to 8 assets (CH-41, CH-47); what the node keeps (CH-18); the health-event budget (41 an hour before P4-4-L6, 45 since) and the byte ceiling (CH-45); an unclean previous run in `sensor.run-loss` (CH-42); and the resume row's `drops_channel` check (CH-13 row 5).

**Shared values under P4-4-L6 (D4-90).** The brief requires that no rule shared with `docs/phase4/D4-c13-0.2-proposal.md` differ in value, name or meaning. These are the values this amendment states that C13 0.2 also states, or must state; each is written here as C13 0.2 states it where C13 already has it, and as D5 proposes to D4 where it is new. **The last column says what #645 at `1c454cb6` states, and what C13 0.2 on `main` (`e20a594f`) had:** "#645 states it" means D4's PR states this note's value, where C13 on `main` stated something else or nothing (as given after "on `main`"); "C13 has it" means this note uses C13's own words (`records-auditor` on #644). #645's §17 maps every row to its clause and finds all 23 the same, and read against #645 at `1c454cb6` this note finds no value, name or meaning that differs:

| | Value | This note | C13 0.2 clause | #645 at `1c454cb6`; C13 on `main` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **`MaxLinkCursorBytes` = 2,048,** for a text form of at most 1,934 bytes, with the `drops_driver` baseline and what is held with W (R and each interface's `packets` and last rise) | §1.4, §1.10 | CH-18, §13 (D5-2), R-5 | #645 states 1,575, without what is held with W (D5's note for D4, on #641); on `main`, 192 bytes in CH-18 and §13, and R-5's "192-byte cursor" (C13 l.1260) |
| 2 | **What the node keeps where:** the cursor holds the position, `write_no`, the run's `config_digest`, Ds, the last `newest_seq`, the heartbeat baselines, W with its heartbeat and causes and what is kept with it (R, and each interface's `packets` and last rise), the run's spans with cut ends and owed marks, and `prior` with its spans and owed mark. `sensorrun.json` holds the run's second copy, SL-30's counts and proofs, the cap's record times, the spent re-arm hashes, the held pool events, the poison record, the standing `sensor-transmitted` and a copy of the earlier of W and R, under `write_no`. `sensors.json` adds the generation at which the position began and `enrolled_at` | §1.3, §1.4 | CH-18, CH-16, CH-40, CH-12 | #645 states it; on `main`, spans, `prior` and the digest in `sensorrun.json` (CH-16, CH-18, CH-40, CH-45's list for D5) |
| 3 | **Any enrolment closes the open link,** a `renew` with the pin unchanged included (`enrolment`) | §1.1, §1.3 | CH-37, CH-11, M-9 | #645 states it; on `main`, "a new enrolment … that replaces the pin" |
| 4 | **A position below the generation at which it began reads as none:** no `taken`, row 3 reads 0 | §1.3 | CH-6, CH-13, CH-18, CH-12 | #645 states it; on `main`, CH-18 decides by "the generation's enrolment reason and pin" |
| 5 | **The dial condition:** the database open, the cursor read and parsed, the intake started; else no dial and `sensor-state-unread` (`position`) | §1.1 | CH-4, CH-18 | #645 states it; on `main`, CH-4 names the position only |
| 6 | **`sensor-state-unread`'s `cause` detail:** `fold-record`, `fold-read`, `position`, `run-file`, and what each does | §1.3 | CH-45, CH-4, CH-11 | #645 states it; on `main`, one name, no cause |
| 7 | **A span's rise is judged against Ds,** the D at which the run's spans were last accepted | §1.6 | CH-40 | #645 states it; on `main`, against the run's `drops_channel` rise |
| 8 | **One cut end for each span;** a part added after a cut span's stated end keeps no window | §1.6 | CH-40 | #645 states it; on `main`, "its new part gets its own cut end" |
| 9 | **Owed marks:** a span's `lost` gap and `prior`'s `sensor.run-loss` owed from the save that accepts them, cleared only after the event commits; twice, never not at all; `sensor.run-loss` carries the run's generation, counter and `run_id` | §1.4, §1.6 | CH-16, CH-40, CH-45 | #645 states it; on `main`, none of it |
| 10 | **No tie for skew, two causes, one rule:** observations marked `node.skewed` and alerts under `clock-skew` while the alarm stands or the latest sample is outside `max_skew_ms`, or the rule on the two clocks refuses a tie (a stored time whose offset of wall from monotonic clock moved more than `max_skew_ms` since it was recorded, or recorded before the node's start; a record captured at or before the capture floor that a move of the sensor's clock against the node's monotonic clock raises, K kept across a sensor's connections; a tie decided no earlier than the first tick read after its record), counted apart, a node start apart again; the key means "no tie for skew"; a sample after a held read loop gates and is counted | §1.5 | CH-22, CH-41 | #645 states it (CH-22, CH-41, CH-47, T-10); on `main`, CH-22 has the mark for both causes (C13 l.686), CH-41's input names the alarm only, and neither the key's meaning nor the count of samples after a held read loop is stated |
| 11 | **The coverage-gap causes, a closed set of nine:** `node-start`, `link`, `restart`, `held`, `revoked`, `sensor-failed`, `lost`, `blind`, and `silent` for a heartbeat silence on a link that stays up (SL-16's ninth cause at #641's `467c1dd6`; D5's token, which CH-45 takes); every gap, opened at a start or while the node runs, starts at the earlier of W and R, R frozen while a gap is open; W and R raised to the `to_utc` of a gap other than `lost` that closes unwatched, and never below it after (the floor), the gap held in the cursor from the raise until its event commits, a later gap closed unwatched meanwhile merged into it, the merge carrying either part's `clock_stepped`; no record of a later gap of the same sensor other than `lost` written while the closing record of a gap other than `lost` waits, and a `closed` record paired with every unpaired `open` record of the same sensor and an equal `from_utc`; W, R and the floor never later than the node's wall clock, the step check run before every time the node takes from it for a gap, for W, R or the floor, or for the copy, and the cap at a start made at the `node-start` gap's close, the gap marked `clock_stepped` at the start where a value is later than the wall clock; the check acting only on a step back of more than `max_skew_ms`, and a step found marking the gaps closed and not yet written; a gap written `open` no later than 60 s after its open on the monotonic clock; the copy never rising while a gap is held for the floor; `lost` its span; a gap with no W the bound; W and R never later than the heartbeat's `at_utc` + E; within a run a gap's length on the monotonic clock; "shows capture" by CH-31's blind rule, flat counts included, for W's heartbeat, the chain's part 2 (per heartbeat, by `at_utc`, from the rises the cursor holds with W; none held fails) and the unwatched close; W, and its reach-back whenever the node holds no W, the database lost or the sensor never watched, at a start or while the node runs (the run file's copy of the earlier of W and R, else `enrolled_at`, `from_bound: true`); a clock stepped back (`clock_stepped: true`, `time.node-stepped`); **a gap held from `enrolled_at` while the node holds no W, cause `link` (`node-start` at a start), written as any `link` gap and closed at the first current heartbeat with `covered: false`** (SL-16 and gap leg 15 at #641's `9d74bb73`); closed as covered only by one run's chain, with its four parts, `HeartbeatGapMax` 30 s and the count, and `lost`, `blind` and `revoked` never by a chain; a gap marked `clock_stepped` never closed as covered; no partial cover (D1's ruling 6837169700 on #641, widened to every break); the event's details and when it is written, **a `lost` gap once, `closed`, with its span's times** (SL-16). **`from_bound`, `clock_stepped` and the writing of `time.node-stepped` once for each step are D5's names and rule,** which CH-45 states; C4 on `main` already defines `time.node-stepped` (`contracts/event/event.go:465`) and nothing writes it yet | §1.9 item 5 | CH-39, CH-45 (SL-16 and C8-7 in #641) | #645 states it; on `main`, CH-45 names `blind` and `lost` only, under one budget, and C13 records no gap for a link drop or a node start |
| 12 | **The budget: 45 an hour** (12 drop-counts, 4 `lost` gaps, 4 other gaps, a pool of 24, 1 mode change), 3 for a retired sensor; a `lost` gap merged only with `lost` gaps | §1.9 item 5, §1.10 | CH-45, §12 | #645 states it; on `main`, 41 an hour, one gap budget for every cause |
| 13 | **`sensor.counters`:** at most 4 an hour as every name, and no sooner than 10 minutes after its last | §1.9 item 5 | CH-45, §12 | C13 has it: each name at most 4 an hour and `sensor.counters` at most once every 10 minutes (C13 l.1115); this note's wording only says which bound binds; #645 the same |
| 14 | **The alarms that write no health event** (status and OS log only), listed | §1.9 item 5 | CH-45 | #645 states it; on `main`, not stated |
| 15 | **`sensor.link_refused`:** once in a stretch and once an hour; a stretch ends at an accepted hello **or a node start** | §1.9 item 6, §1.10 | CH-11, CH-45 (C8-5 in #641) | #645 states it; on `main`, the node start is not stated |
| 16 | **Standalone cursor saves:** after a hold save, a refusal, a drop, a duplicate or a gap, at most once a second | §1.4 | CH-17 | C13 has it; #645 the same |
| 17 | **The `ack` above both figures** names the higher of the highest seq read and the latest `sent_seq` | §1.4 | CH-17, A-3 | #645 states it (CH-17, A-3); on `main`, CH-17 has it and A-3 says the lower |
| 18 | **`sensor-transmitted`:** any non-zero `frames_outgoing` with no baseline held, any rise against it, late heartbeats included; raised by a run file lost, unreadable or behind. **`sensor-egress-absent`:** the run's first heartbeat seen from its start sets the baseline and raises nothing; a heartbeat that omits the field with no baseline held and that is not that first heartbeat raises it. **Capture loss:** a rise of `drops_driver`, `drops_holder` or `frames_overlong` against its durable baseline, named in `capture_loss`; with no baseline held, the run total counts as the rise (D1's one rule, comment 6840188059 on #645) | §1.8 | CH-26, CH-27, CH-45, W-5 | #645 states it; on `main`, a run's first heartbeat, no durable baseline |
| 19 | **`closed-before-hello`** under `link_closes`, never `run-complete`; CH-38's "run finished" needs a hello accepted on that connection | §1.1 | CH-11's counts, CH-38 | #645 states it; on `main`, not stated |
| 20 | **The egress state in status** reads `unknown` when the field is not carried, D3's word (was "not carried" here) | Part 2 §6 | CH-27 | #645 states it; on `main`, not stated |
| 21 | **The lost-database hello:** with the run file holding a copy of the run, `node-hello` names it with `taken.seq` 0; without one, no `taken`. **The copy carries its generation, and a copy from below the generation at which the position began reads as none** (SL-R6). SL-30's counts read as spent, so the dial waits for a re-arm; status names the position as taken from the run file. **Aligned with SL-31 at `41a073f2`,** by D1's ruling (comment 6837755868 on #644) | §1.4 | CH-6, CH-13, CH-18 | #645 states it; on `main`, not stated |

**From C4 0.4 (D4; D5's B6 on #634, D1 in D4-90):** what C13 0.2 requires of `sensor.counters`, its counters and the detail that marks a capture loss, and nothing else. The node builds `sensor.counters` to it, and the size test of §1.9 item 5 covers the longer event. Nothing else in this note needs C4: `sensor.coverage-gap`'s details and `sensor.run-loss`'s run identity are details of a health event, which C4 0.3 accepts as free keys (`contracts/event/validate.go`; only `sensor.counters` has a closed set).

**From C5: no change to classes or quotas, and none to the store's format.** The design uses the source cursor and the store's `SaveSourceCursor`. SL-31's spans, `prior`, the digest and the write number go in the cursor, a BLOB that C5 does not bound beyond the record's bytes (`contracts/store/commit.go`; D5 on #641, comment 6836978459), so no table and no C5 minor is needed. **D1 is asked to confirm** that C5 0.9 allows more than one database holding the network classes, and a database set kept apart from the runtime's own list (§1.9 item 1), which is the node's layout and not C5's.

**From C7 0.11 (D3):** the two kinds and C7-1 to C7-7 as `docs/phase4/D3-sensor-link.md` §11.1 writes them, with C7-4's table of reason and pin, which the node checks (§1.3).

**From C8 0.13 (D3):** C8-1 to C8-9 as §11.2 of D3's note writes them: the three actions; `sensor.enrolled`'s seven reasons in their order and `previous_state`; `sensor.revoked` always `succeeded`, with `applied_at` and a possibly empty `streams`; the cap keyed by the sensor, the generation and the reason; and the relayed `collector.request`'s sensor fields (C8-6). **C8-8's sensor rules run in a verifier of their own,** beside `VerifyChain` as `VerifyConfigFrom` does, in which a reference to a record before the view is unknown and not a break. They must not run inside the one chain check at the node's start, whose break switches off every asset's event baselines (`newEventLife`, `desk`): the node's sensor consumer can let the audit stream be pruned past a `collector.request`, and a `sensor.revoked` written late would otherwise end configuration-change detection for every asset.

**From D3's `modules/security/nodekey`:** the link key's file and its reader, NK-1 to NK-6 (§1.1, §1.9 item 11), in place of the kind of its own that this note first asked for (ruling 1 on #631 (comment 6804993239)). **From D6:** its three tests that call `ServeSensor` move with the third part. **From D7:** the two release floors of §1.15 (ruling 14 on #628; ruling 7 on #631 (comment 6804993239)), no floor for the key register, and the release blocker F1 to F11 (§1.0).

## 1.12 What applying `node.network_baseline_ack` takes (for the third part's brief; not built)

SL-R5's "acknowledged again" and the baseline legs of SL-T10 wait on it. What it takes:

1. **The network learner on the node.** `netdetect`'s learner runs, its draft is checkpointed (`netcheckpoint.learner`), and a frozen draft is recorded as `baseline.network` on `netbaseline/<sensor_id>`. Without a recorded draft `netack.Adopt` refuses every acknowledgement.
2. **The relay.** `node.network_baseline_ack` joins `relayKinds` and `nodeActKinds` (`adminrelay.go`), and `applyAdminPart` gains its case, bound to the install key as `node.baseline_ack` is (`admin_actions.go`).
3. **The node's part.** `policy.apply` with `admin_keys` first when `netack.NeedsGrantRecord` says so. The latest draft, and `network-baseline-<sensor_id>.json` from `admin_dir` under the protected-file rule, passed to `netack.Adopt`. C8 `config.baseline_acknowledged` of kind `network` written before anything is stored, `denied` with `Adopt`'s reason when it refuses. The accepted bytes stored as `baseline.network`, evidence, never removed.
4. **The detector takes it.** `DetectorConfig` gains the accepted baseline (`detectorConfig`, `netdetector.go`), the detector's checkpoint names the baseline it judged with, and a newly accepted one starts a new detector half, as the event baselines do.
5. **SL-R5 itself.** An acknowledgement before the latest `sensor.revoked` of its sensor is not in force. The detector then runs as before acknowledgement, and `sensor-baseline-void` stands while that sensor feeds. A re-key keeps it (SL-R6).
6. **Tests.** The `netack` cases already in `modules/security/netack`, run end to end through the relay; SL-T10's baseline legs; a revocation voids, and a re-key does not.

## 1.13 Choices left

Rulings 1 to 15 settle the choices of the first text. What is still open:

- **For the owner, through D1:** O-12 on PRP (one host with two ports, two hosts, or one LAN only; a second sensor waits on it, ruling 1); O-6, the network between node and sensor (a cable of its own needs a free port on the node's hardware for each sensor, not checked for any machine); and O-10, the time source, on which the skew bound's assumption rests.

## 1.14 Limits

- **Nothing in Part 1 is built or has run.** Every test named is a plan.
- **Assumptions, each marked where used:** that Go's TLS client sends no early data, and that no session cache means no resumption (`TestDialNoResumption` decides); that Windows' default outbound policy is allow (§1.1); that a site clock keeps within a second, which rests on O-10 (§1.5); that a station LAN is never quiet for `blind_after_s` (§1.8, A7).
- **Unmeasured figures, each a choice with its reason:** the health record's 4,096 bytes; `HoldLockWait`; the detector's drive bounds; the late index's horizon and entry size.
- **After a break longer than `backlog_tie_s`,** the sessions in it alert under one aggregated key: D1's fail-closed choice (§1.6).
- **`sensors.json` and `sensorrun.json`** share `suspensions.json`'s open question about a rename's durability on Windows (D4-65); §1.3's cursor rule turns a rolled-back `sensors.json` into a correct fold or an unknown state, and the write number turns a rolled-back `sensorrun.json` into an unreadable one (§1.1).
- **Assumption, not shown:** that a commit of a sensor database survives a power cut on Windows, because the store appends to its write-ahead log at `synchronous=FULL` and renames nothing on a commit (`platform/store/db.go`, `desk`). D5 read neither the SQLite documentation nor the driver: whether its Windows file layer flushes the folder when it creates the `-wal` file, and whether a FULL commit's flush gets through the disk's cache, are open, and no power cut has been run (H-14, C-22). **A database's creation does rename** (`<path>.new` into place, then `syncDir`, which does nothing on Windows), so a sensor database created shortly before a power cut can come back absent: that is "lost where a position existed" (§1.4), not a stale cursor.
- **The cursor's cost:** every stored observation carries it, at most 838 bytes with no span or `prior` held and up to 1,934 while they are (§1.4).
- **After a lost sensor database:** `sensor-gap-undeclared` is raised once for the acknowledged records the agent had trimmed (§1.4); the start gap reaches back to the run file's copy of the earlier of W and R, or to the live enrolment's `enrolled_at`, which is a bound and may be long before the outage (`from_bound: true`; §1.9 item 5); a `prior` owed in it may be lost, and status names it.
- **Fail-loud doubles, stated:** a kill between an owed event's commit and the cursor save that clears its mark writes the event twice (§1.4, §1.6); with no baseline held, a transmit already raised can be raised again and a capture loss counted twice (§1.8).
- **Volatile:** the split of drop and refusal totals by reason and asset since the last `intake.drop-counted` event; up to a minute of held pool event counts, and those held in a run file that is then lost or behind (named in status).
- **`recover` catches a panic, not a fatal runtime error** (§1.9 item 9).
- **`C` is a lower bound:** the platform class's other writers are not counted (§1.9 item 5).
- **Not designed here:** a merge of two sensors' records; the second sensor itself (ruling 1); the sensor; its update path; a central role.

## 1.15 C13 0.2's points D5-1 to D5-12, as ruled, and the 0.1 rollback

| | Point | D5's answer, with D1's rulings on #627 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D5-1 | Final outcomes | A C13 refusal, and a drop by the unresolved budget, at a full class or at the record cap. Any other store error closes the connection with `store` and is not taken (§1.4) |
| D5-2 | Durable counts | The run's refused and dropped totals in the cursor, with the commit or a cursor save, before the `ack` passes them (ruling 2 on #627; §1.4) |
| D5-3 | What the node keeps | Generation, counter, `run_id`, T, G, D and the two totals in the cursor; **since P4-4-L6 also, in the cursor, `prior` until its event commits, the run's `lost` spans, its `config_digest`, Ds, the heartbeat baselines, W and `write_no`** (SL-31; §1.4); in `sensorrun.json`, the run's second copy and SL-30's count and proof for each pair (§1.1), under `write_no` (CH-18) |
| D5-4 | The expected `config_digest` | `sensors[].config_digest`, read at the sensor's console at commissioning; a mismatch raises `sensor-coverage-mismatch` only. A patch of the sensor then needs a configuration edit and a restart, and the alarm stands until then |
| D5-5 | `pinned_by` | Status, with a standing alarm for `console` and no refusal (ruling 4) |
| D5-6 | The dialer | CH-4, CH-9, CH-10 and CH-38 (`RedialFloor` 10 s), with the node-local connect and write deadlines (§1.1) |
| D5-7 | The hold and `newest_seq` | Withdrawn on #627: the late index (§1.6), under the break rule. `newest_seq` serves status, `sensor-buffer-filling` and the end of a backlog |
| D5-8 | Reachability | Only an accepted frame resets the timer; `sensor-no-heartbeat` while a backlog drains (§1.8, CH-44) |
| D5-9 | Health events; the intake reader | No event for each heartbeat; `sensor.counters` and `sensor.run-loss` under the caps (§1.9 item 5), `sensor.run-loss` also for an unclean previous run (ruling 4 on #627). The intake checkpoint keeps `otsec.netcheckpoint.intake/0.1`: a held body is raw JSON (`HeldRecord.Observation`) decoded with `json.Unmarshal` (`Link.record`) |
| D5-10 | Sensors per node | The list shape, at most two, one built (ruling 1) |
| D5-11 | Names, bounds, caps | §1.10, and the caps of §1.9 item 5 |
| D5-12 | Two-way `ServeSensor` | §1.1. The read loop is synchronous, so `node-hello` goes out only after the previous connection's records are finished or untaken |

**The 0.1 rollback** (CH-35, ruling 14). `desk`: `Runtime.detectorCheckpoints` (`platform/node/netdetector.go`) keeps only documents whose `schema` equals `netobs.CheckpointIdentifier`, which `contracts/netobs/checkpoint.go` defines as `otsec.netcheckpoint/0.1`. So a 0.1 node does not see a 0.2 checkpoint at all. It starts as if for the first time, with no alarm, raises the alerts of the retained observations again and stores them a second time, and never removes the 0.2 document, which can be 1,354,752 bytes in the configuration class. The start-up rules allow a rollback: the detector starts in the manager, not in `Start`, and C5 checks a configuration document only at its commit (`platform/store/commit.go`). What makes CH-35 hold:

1. **A reader build, released on its own before any build that writes 0.2.** Its `detectorCheckpoints` recognises any `otsec.netcheckpoint/0.x` detector document, raises `network-detector-not-restored` for a minor it cannot read, starts from the oldest retained observation, and removes the document once its own first checkpoint has committed. **It is a release rule** (D6): D7's record names that build as the lowest a 0.2 node may roll back to, and the 0.2 writer ships no earlier than the release after it. A rollback to an older build gets the silent restart above.
2. **In the same build, the tie goes into a held observation's raw JSON** instead of a re-encoding with the build's own type (D4), so a rolled-back node keeps `made_utc` and `late` in the held bodies it commits.
3. **The duplicate alerts stay a stated limit.** Reading `last_alert_seq` alone out of a document the build cannot otherwise read would let a malformed document hold real alerts back, which suppresses; a duplicate fails loud. So the reader build raises the retained observations' alerts again, and says so.
4. **The configuration rolls back with the binary,** because the node decodes its configuration strictly (`platform/node/config.go`): a build from before the third part refuses a `network_sensor` section that carries the new fields (`sensors`, `link_key_ref`), so a rollback must restore the earlier configuration too, and D7's record names the configuration shape beside the build (NK-6; D5 on #631).
5. **The test:** in the reader build, a 0.2 detector checkpoint written from #627's text as a fixture; the node raises `network-detector-not-restored`, starts, and removes the document after its first checkpoint. Its control is `main`'s exact match, which raises nothing.
6. **A second rollback floor: the C8 0.13 reader** (C8-1; ruling 7 on #631 (comment 6804993239)). A 0.12 build refuses a `sensor.*` record and a `collector.request` with a sensor target, and the scheduler takes an audit record it cannot parse as a bad record and holds every run (`asset-suspensions-unknown`; `platform/node/suspend.go`, `desk`). So a rollback below the 0.13 reader stops SER collection on that node, not only the sensor. D7 records both builds, the checkpoint reader's and the C8 0.13 reader's, as release floors. **No floor for the key register:** its schema does not change (NK-6).
7. **No floor for C4 0.4** (C4 0.4 §3). A 0.3 build reads every stored 0.4 event, and refuses only a pending one (`commit.go` l.353). No pending C4 event outlives the process that built it, on `main` or in this design, since held pool events are kept as counts and built at their write (§1.9 item 5). So a rollback from 0.4 code loses nothing while that holds, and D7's record names that condition beside the build.

| 2 | The link key in a file of its own, read by the sensor manager after `Start`, any fault holding back the sensor links only (NK-1 to NK-6); NK-2's input is the loaded register's public keys, and with `key_register` set and not loaded the link is held back | §1.1, §1.2 (`link_key_ref`), §1.11 |
| 3 | SL-30's dial limit: count and proof per pair in `sensorrun.json`, saved and synced before each dial; a failed save forbids the dial; an unreadable count reads as spent unless a cursor of the live enrolment's generation proves the pair; the proof saved with the new run; the count outlives the entry and the `sensor_id`; a refused connection counted; `sensor-dial-limit`; the re-arm by `otsec sensor-redial`, refusing a file older than the spent set's last rebuild | §1.1, §1.10 |
